## Pore-Scale Characterization of Carbonate Rock Heterogeneity and Prediction of Petrophysical Properties

- ID: SCA2012-58, year: 2012
- Authors: Chenchen Wang, Jun Yao, Kejian Wu, Yongfei Yang, Xin Wang
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: multiscale digital rock, MCMC reconstruction, pore network model, carbonate heterogeneity, micro porosity connectivity, superposition method
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2012/SCA2012-58.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2012-58)

Macro- and micro-pore 3D digital rocks reconstructed from SEM images by MCMC are superposed; the combined network gives higher permeability and lower residual oil than either scale alone.

Carbonates have pore sizes spanning orders of magnitude, so single-resolution images miss either macro or micro pore structure. Two 2D SEM thin-section images (1.34 µm and 0.335 µm resolution) were used to build 3D macro and micro pore digital rocks by MCMC reconstruction, then superposed into one multiscale rock of 400³ voxels with porosity 0.519 (versus 0.254 and 0.355). Pore networks extracted with PATs were compared in geometry, topology and water-wet invasion-percolation flow. The superposition network's properties lay between those of the two single-scale networks. Its absolute permeability was 15.2 mD versus 9.56 mD (macro) and 4.45 mD (micro), and imbibition residual oil saturation was 0.14 versus 0.28 and 0.20, attributed to micro-porosity improving connectivity.

## Computed Two-Phase Relative Permeability Using Digital Rock Physics in a Shale Formation

- ID: SCA2013-037, year: 2013
- Authors: Michael Suhrer, Jonas Toelke, Elizabeth Diaz, Avrami Grader, Joel Walls, Dora P. Restrepo, Maria T. Cantisano, Sandra Cespedes
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: digital rock physics, Lattice-Boltzmann, shale relative permeability, FIB-SEM imaging, wettability contact angle, viscosity ratio
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2013/SCA2013-037.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2013-037)

Lattice-Boltzmann simulations on FIB-SEM images of Colombian shale give oil-water and gas-water relative permeability, showing strong wettability effects and a smaller, unusual viscosity-ratio effect.

The paper estimates absolute and relative permeability of an organic-rich Colombian shale using digital rock physics. Samples were imaged by low-energy SEM and 3D FIB-SEM (10-15 nm voxels), segmented into pores, organic matter and minerals, and 44 samples were used for porosity-permeability trends. Six samples (porosity 6.5-14.3%, permeability 300-12,000 nD) were chosen for Lattice-Boltzmann two-phase flow, with pore-to-throat size ratio around 3. Oil-water imbibition runs on fully oil-saturated rock at contact angles of 54° and 135° and varied API/viscosity ratios showed wettability strongly changes curve shape and endpoints, while higher viscosity ratio gave lower krw and higher kro. Gas-water runs gave critical gas saturation of 0.14 (W1) and 0.18 (W2) with Srw of 30% and 40%. The authors note simplifications such as uniform wettability and fully saturated initial conditions.

## Kozeny'S Equation for Better Core Analysis

- ID: SCA2013-048, year: 2013
- Authors: Pudji Permadi, Indonesia Andy Setyo Wibowo
- Method: theory or analytical model
- Keywords: Kozeny equation, rock typing, Leverett J-function, hydraulic radius, micro-fractures, permeability prediction
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2013/SCA2013-048.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2013-048)

Dimensional analysis of the Kozeny equation yields a power-law with exponent b and efficiency a, enabling rock typing of carbonates and sandstones and micro-fracture identification.

The paper recasts the Kozeny equation as a power law relating (k/φ)^0.5 to k/φ³, with exponent b (ideal 0.5) and pore efficiency a (ideal 1.0). Routine core, petrographic and capillary pressure data from a reefal carbonate (1.3–27.5% porosity, 0.001–1800 mD) and a sandstone (1.6–18.8%, 0.007–16.9 mD) were grouped by petrographic features and plotted on a type curve. All groups fall below the ideal line, with a from 0.553 to 0.242 and b from 0.405 to 0.245 for the carbonate, and a from 0.380 to 0.180 and b from 0.345 to 0.225 for the sandstone. Lower b corresponds to poorer rock quality and reflects depositional and diagenetic similarity. The groups are distinguishable on Leverett J-function curves, and combining the power law with Swirr–k correlations gives rock-type-dependent permeability prediction. Samples with active micro-fractures show lower porosity but higher permeability and can be separated by a dividing line on the plot.

## Pore Scale Modeling of Brine Dependent Permeability

- ID: SCA2013-064, year: 2013
- Authors: Janne Pedersen, Espen Jettestuen, Jan Ludvig Vinningland, Merete V. Madland, Aksel Hiorth
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: lattice Boltzmann method, moving boundary routine, volume of fluid, Peclét-Damköhler number, Carman-Kozeny permeability, mineral precipitation
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2013/SCA2013-064.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2013-064)

Three lattice Boltzmann moving boundary routines for mineral growth are compared for mass conservation, rotational invariance and convergence; at high PeDa, surface area growth drives permeability loss more than porosity loss.

Permeability loss in chalk flooded with seawater is linked to precipitation and dissolution, so pore-scale methods need reliable moving pore surfaces. The authors compare three volume-of-fluid moving boundary routines in a D2Q9 BGK lattice Boltzmann model, simulating growth of a mineral seed in a diffusion field at different Peclét-Damköhler (PeDa) numbers. All three conserve mass, but deviation from rotational invariance grows with PeDa; the subgrid VOF method (method 3) deviates least. A surface tension term gives convergence with grid refinement, with deviation falling roughly fourfold when resolution doubles. Using Carman-Kozeny, permeability can approach zero at high PeDa without significant porosity loss because specific surface area rises sharply.

## The Rev Challenge – Estimating Representative Elementary Volumes and Porous Rock Inhomogeneity from High Resolution Micro-CT Data Sets

- ID: SCA2013-069, year: 2013
- Authors: Matthias Halisch
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: representative elementary volume, micro-CT, porosity variogram, inhomogeneity factor, Bentheimer sandstone, digital rock
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2013/SCA2013-069.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2013-069)

Extends classical micro-CT REV estimation with directional porosity variogram analysis and a new inhomogeneity factor, tested on Bentheimer sandstone, linking inhomogeneity minima to grains, dissolved feldspar and clay.

Classical REV estimation from porosity alone ignores directional variations and small-scale inhomogeneity. The study uses a high-resolution µ-CT data set of Bentheimer sandstone (1.75 µm voxels, maximum cube of 1750 voxels) and extracts random non-overlapping subvolumes of growing edge length (50–1750 voxels), comparing porosity with helium pycnometry. Porosity stabilizes beyond about 1000 voxels and the REV cut-off is set at 1250 voxels, with CT porosity slightly underestimated because of segmentation. Variograms of slice-by-slice directional porosity logs show that the y- and z-directions reach saturation, whereas the x-direction does not. A new inhomogeneity factor, IPhi, built from directional porosity standard deviations, shows minima at 300, 750 and 1325 voxels, which are linked to quartz grain size, spacing of dissolved feldspar and spacing of clay agglomerations.

## Core-Based Petrophysical Rock Classification by Quantifying Pore-System Orthogonality with a Bimodal Gaussian Density Function

- ID: SCA2013-079, year: 2013
- Authors: Chicheng Xu, Carlos Torres-Verdín
- Method: data analysis or correlation
- Keywords: bimodal Gaussian pore-size distribution, mercury injection capillary pressure, pore-system orthogonality, Thomeer hyperbola, tight-gas sandstone, NMR T2 spectra
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2013/SCA2013-079.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2013-079)

Fits MICP pore-size distributions with bimodal Gaussian functions and clusters pore-system orthogonality matrices to classify Bossier tight-gas sandstone rock types consistently across porosity-permeability, MICP and NMR data.

Conventional hydraulic-radius rock typing fails to capture the petrophysical variability of tight-gas sandstones with complex pore structure. The authors compare Gaussian and Thomeer-derivative pore-size distribution models fitted to MICP data, finding Gaussian parameters relate more directly to pore volume, connectivity and size uniformity. They define a pore-system orthogonality measure from the six bimodal Gaussian attributes, compute N×N orthogonality matrices for large and small pore modes, and cluster them to define rock types. Tested on a Bossier tight-gas sand key well (over 100 plugs with porosity 2–10 p.u. and permeability 0.001–1 mD; 20 plugs with MICP to 60,000 psi and NMR), the rock types show clear porosity-permeability trends. NMR T2 peaks shift to lower T2 as rock type number increases (quality decreases), confirming consistent ranking across data types.

## Multi-Scale Imaging and Cross-Property Correlations in Heterogenous Sandstone

- ID: SCA2014-021, year: 2014
- Authors: Pieter W.S.K. Botha, Alexandra N. Golab, Pradeep Bhattad, Carley Goodwin, Adrian P. Sheppard
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: micro-CT multi-scale imaging, image unitisation, Lattice-Boltzmann permeability, formation factor, grain sorting, cementation exponent
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-021.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-021)

Multi-resolution μCT of a heterogeneous Precipice sandstone plug is unitised and simulated, showing cross-resolution correlations of permeability and formation factor with porosity, sorting and pore size.

The paper addresses predicting core-plug scale petrophysical properties in heterogeneous sandstone when low-resolution μCT cannot resolve pore throats. A 25 mm plug (PU7, Precipice sandstone) was imaged at ~16 μm/voxel and an 8 mm sub-plug at ~5 μm/voxel, registered, and automatically unitised from grain size and porosity logs into 18 and 3 units respectively. Grain and pore size, sorting, phase fractions, Lattice-Boltzmann permeability and formation factor were computed per unit. Grain and pore sizes from the two resolutions correlate linearly, with the 25 mm data slightly overestimating sizes. Log(k) from the 8 mm image correlates positively with porosity and negatively with sorting and log(F) in both datasets, supporting cross-resolution prediction; some simulated permeabilities exceed 8 Darcy and await experimental verification.

## X-Ray Micro-CT Assisted Drainage Rock Typing for Characterization of Flow Behaviour of Laminated Sandstone Reservoirs

- ID: SCA2014-024, year: 2014
- Authors: Pradeep Bhattad, Benjamin Young, Carl Fredrik Berg, Alf Birger Rustad, Olivier Lopez
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: micro-CT, laminated sandstone, centrifuge drainage rock typing, permeability upscaling, Katz-Thompson, image registration
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 2
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-024.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-024)

Centrifuge drainage of CT-imaged laminated sandstone plugs maps open and tight laminae in 3D; sub-plug properties are upscaled, matching measured vertical permeability for one plug but not the other.

Laminated sandstone plugs are difficult to analyse with routine or special core analysis because several rock types occur within one plug. Two NCS plugs were imaged by micro-CT dry, saturated with NaI brine, and after centrifuge drainage at 4500 rpm (3 µm throat radius), with image registration separating drained (open) from residual-brine (tight) lamina. Sub-plugs of 3-5 mm were imaged at about 3 µm, with LBM permeability for open rock and Katz-Thompson for tight rock, and sister sub-plugs gave helium porosity and MICP. Image porosity matched well for open rock, but tight rock was underestimated (sample 235: 15.4-16.3% vs 27.4% helium) because of sub-resolution pores and dense minerals. Upscaled vertical permeability agreed reasonably for plug 235 (0.27 vs 0.12 mD) but overestimated plug 254 (17.7 vs 3.25 mD), likely because within-lamina property variation was ignored.

## Coupling Multiphase Hydrodynamic and NMR Pore-Scale Modeling for Advanced Characterization of Saturated Rocks

- ID: SCA2014-026, year: 2014
- Authors: N. Evseev, O. Dinariev, M. Hürlimann, S. Safonov
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: density functional hydrodynamics, NMR D-T2 maps, Bloch-Torrey equations, pore-scale waterflooding, wettability, digital rock
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-026.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-026)

Density functional hydrodynamics simulation of drainage and waterflooding is coupled with Bloch-Torrey NMR modeling on digital sandstones, producing D-T2 maps for water-wet, mixed-wet and oil-wet cases.

The paper couples a density functional hydrodynamics pore-scale simulator (DHD) with a numerical solver of the generalized Bloch-Torrey equations to link fluid distributions to NMR response. Three digital rock models (Berea, Achimov, Cenomanian sandstone) were taken through oil drainage to Swi and waterflooding to 98% water cut, using nonuniform contact-angle maps. Berea (water-wet) gave Swi 0.302 and Sor 0.253 with fast water breakthrough, Achimov (mixed-wet) gave Swi 0.226 and Sor 0.224 with slower water cut rise, and oil-wet Cenomanian gave Swi 0.098 and Sor 0.351 with the longest production. D-T2 maps before and after waterflooding show how wettability and surface relaxivity shift water and oil signals. The authors propose using such simulations with known fluid configurations to calibrate NMR inversion and logging interpretation.

## 3D Mapping of Pore and Organic Matter Distributions in Unconventional Reservoirs Utilizing a Digital Rocks Approach

- ID: SCA2014-027, year: 2014
- Authors: Andrew Fogden, Jill Middleton, Thomas McKay, Shane Latham, Rohini Marathe, Michael Turner, Adrian Sheppard, James J. Howard, F. David Lane
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: micro-CT, diiodomethane contrast, iodine staining organic matter, unconventional carbonate, digital rock, image registration
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-027.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-027)

Micro-CT of mini-plugs saturated with diiodomethane and stained with iodine yields registered 3D maps of porosity and organic matter in carbonate-rich unconventional rocks, matching plug porosity and TOC within 20%.

Fine-grained unconventional rocks are hard to characterize by routine core analysis, and micro-CT cannot normally separate pore from organic matter (OM). Four carbonate-rich samples (S1-S4, porosity 4.3-7.6%, TOC 1.8-5.6 wt%) were cored as 3 mm mini-plugs and scanned at 2 µm/voxel in four states: initial, diiodomethane-saturated at 20 MPa, post-dry, and iodine-stained. Registered differences gave 3D porosity and OM maps, and scatter histograms of the two differences classified features as mineral, matrix, porous-only, OM-only, OM-hosted porosity or partings. Segmented porosity (4.5-9.0%) and organic volume (4.0-11.8%) agreed within about 20% with plug measurements, less well for the more laminated S3 and S4. Registered FE-SEM of an ion-milled S1 face confirmed that the contrast differences track micro/nano-porosity and OM. Plug-scale permeability, resistivity and velocity showed anisotropy strongly affected by coring-induced partings.

## An Automated Machine-Learning Procedure for Robust Classification of SEM Images of Cross-Laminated Sandstones for Digital Rock Analysis

- ID: SCA2014-034, year: 2014
- Authors: Chen Jin, Jingsheng Ma
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: SEM image classification, cross-laminated sandstone, random forest, WEKA, multi-scale image integration, tight sandstone
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-034.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-034)

An automated procedure combining unsupervised labelling of random templates and supervised random-forest classification identifies laminae in a high-resolution SEM image of a cross-laminated tight sandstone.

Identifying laminae in high-resolution SEM images is needed to calibrate low-resolution images in multi-scale digital rock reconstruction of cross-laminated sandstones. The authors propose a procedure: rescale the image so the thinnest lamina is resolved, choose a template size by minimizing the coefficient of variation, randomly sample templates (100 here), label them without supervision, then classify the whole image with a supervised random forest. It was implemented in ImageJ with the WEKA plugin on a 17328x26378 pixel SEM image (about 1.1 µm) of a Triassic tight red sandstone. The image was rescaled to 450x712 pixels with 12x12 templates and 9 features, and three classes were identified. The result distinguished both high-contrast mineralogical laminae and weakly contrasting laminae or within-lamina patterns. Pixel-wise agreement between classifications using different third features was only about 70-79%, suggesting more or better features could improve classification.

## Sulige Gas Field in Changqing Oilfield China

- ID: SCA2014-036, year: 2014
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: representative elementary volume, micro-CT digital rock, pore network model, tight sandstone, coefficient of variation, Sulige gas field
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-036.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-036)

Micro-CT pore network models of Sulige tight sandstone show porosity, permeability, pore radius and throat length stabilize at a 300^3 voxel (1020 µm) REV.

The paper determines the representative elementary volume (REV) of tight sandstone from the Sulige Gas Field, Changqing Oilfield, China, which is needed to scale pore-scale results to Darcy-scale properties. A sample was imaged by micro-CT (Zeiss MCT-400) at 3.4 µm resolution, and non-overlapping subvolumes from 100^3 to 600^3 voxels were filtered (non-local means), segmented (watershed) and converted into pore network models. Porosity, average pore radius, throat length, coordination number and network permeability were computed for each size. Porosity fell from 4.63% (100^3) to about 2.5% at 300^3 and above, and permeability settled near 13×10^-3 µm². The standard deviation and coefficient of variation also dropped sharply above 300^3, with the coefficient of variation near 5% or below for porosity and permeability. Both approaches give a REV of 300^3 voxels, about 1020 µm per side.

## On Diverse Applications of Qemscan in the Oil and Gas Industry (and Beyond)

- ID: SCA2014-040, year: 2014
- Authors: Theis I. Sølling, Kristian Mogensen, Tobias Gerwig
- Method: measurement technique or apparatus
- Keywords: QEMScan, automated mineralogy, species identification protocol, sample preparation, cuttings analysis, 2D porosity
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-040.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-040)

Shows how QEMScan automated mineralogy, with tailored SIP libraries and careful sample preparation, minimizes unclassified pixels across carbonates, shales, cuttings, salts and archaeological samples.

QEMScan maps 2D mineralogy by matching low-count X-ray spectra to a species identification protocol (SIP) library, and unclassified pixels (0-70% in the authors' experience) are the main concern. Using an FEI 650F with two EDS detectors at 15 keV, the authors examine thin sections and polished epoxy-mounted cuttings. Carbonate thin sections needed no SIP changes, giving about 0.18% unclassified pixels and 21-26% porosity consistent with MICP. A shale case with 70% unclassified pixels under the standard library was resolved by slightly raising Fe and Mg and lowering Al and Si acceptance in the SIP. Careful cleaning and polishing sharply reduced unclassified pixels in cuttings. Further applications include amorphous salt precipitate identification, porosity versus depth, particle size distributions and pottery provenance.

## Calibration of digital pore scale models

- ID: SCA2014-048, year: 2014
- Authors: V. Ravlo, K.S. Årland
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: digital rock analysis, micro-porosity, multi-scale imaging, mercury injection capillary pressure, process-based modeling, kaolinite
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 4
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-048.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-048)

Digital rock models of a North Sea sandstone are calibrated against routine porosity, permeability and MICP data, with multi-scale micro-porosity models bringing simulated Swi to 14% versus 12% measured.

Digital rock analysis struggles with samples containing sub-resolution micro-porosity, so models must be calibrated against experimental data. A 5 mm mini-plug from the Norwegian continental shelf was imaged by micro-CT at 3 µm/voxel, segmented into pores, micro-porous phase and solid, and compared with routine porosity and permeability; dry/wet registration gave total porosity in good agreement. Single-scale network drainage simulations matched MICP only at entry pressure and high water saturation, reaching just 0.6 bar and about 50% Sw, versus about 12% Sw at 5 bar experimentally. Process-based models of kaolinite aggregates, built from BSEM data and validated against MCT and FIB-SEM networks, represented the micro-porous phase and were upscaled with steady-state methods. The integrated multi-scale model gave Swi of 14% versus 12% experimental, and the authors stress comparing digital and experimental data at identical scales and calibrating wettability from fluid distributions.

## Carbonate Reservoir Rock Typing and the Link Between Routine Core Analysis and Special Core Analysis

- ID: SCA2014-049, year: 2014
- Authors: Nadege Bize-Forest, Victoria Baines, Austin Boyd, Adam Moss, Rodolfo Oliveira
- Method: data analysis or correlation
- Keywords: flow zone index, reservoir rock typing, self-organizing map, mercury injection capillary pressure, Archie cementation exponent, carbonates
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-049.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-049)

Fifty carbonate samples are classified into five FZI rock types, with the number of types set by a self-organizing map, and linked to MICP, Archie m and SWir.

The paper classifies fifty carbonate samples (22 limestones, 28 dolostones) from over fifty reservoirs into reservoir rock types using the Flow Zone Index method. An unsupervised self-organizing map using MICP parameters, porosity, permeability and water saturation indicates that five clusters best describe the data. FZI ranges are defined from key permeability values at 10% porosity (0.1, 1, 10, 50, 100 mD), giving five RRTs with FZI boundaries from 0.3 to about 12 µm. Higher FZI rock types show sharper capillary pressure transition zones, lower entry pressure and larger pore throats. Archie m ranges 1.8-2.5, decreasing with porosity in limestones and increasing in dolostones, which is explained by pore texture. Each RRT has its own RQI-porosity regression, and mean SWir ranges from 35% in RRT1 to 13% in RRT5.

## Capillary Pressure and Relative Permeability Assessment on Whole Core Samples from a Giant Middle Eastern Carbonate Reservoir Utilizing Digital Rock Physics

- ID: SCA2014-058, year: 2014
- Authors: Safouh Koronfol, Avrami Grader, Michael Suhrer, Jonas Toelke, Yaoming Mu, Moustafa Dernaika, Mahendra Pratap, Mohammad Al Hammadi, Ahmad Al Ratrout, Zubair Kalam
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: digital rock physics, whole core, relative permeability, primary drainage capillary pressure, stylolite, Lattice Boltzmann, upscaling
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 3
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-058.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-058)

Digital rock physics computed drainage Pc and Kr on four carbonate whole cores and their composite, matching lab plug data and showing the stylolite layer does not block flow.

Four whole cores from two Middle Eastern carbonate formations, separated by a stylolite layer, were evaluated with digital rock physics (DRP) for primary drainage Pc and imbibition and secondary drainage Kr. The workflow used whole core dual-energy CT, multi-scale micro-CT (down to 0.5 µm) and nano-CT (0.05 µm) imaging, Lattice Boltzmann Pc computation, and up-scaling to whole core and composite scale. Results were compared blind against conventional lab data on plugs and plug composites. Pc and Kr matched reasonably, except the stylolite imbibition Krw, where the lab end point was 0.68 against 0.26 from DRP. Composite simulations with two stylolite wettability scenarios (contact angle 130° versus 30°) showed the stylolite layer is permeable and does not act as a barrier. The lab work took six years, whereas the DRP took six months.

## Displacement Mechanisms in Single and Multiple Pores from Lattice Boltzmann Simulations and Micro-Fluidic Experiments

- ID: SCA2014-060, year: 2014
- Authors: Edo S. Boek, Jianhui Yang, Emily M. Chapman, John P. Crawshaw
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: lattice Boltzmann, micro-fluidic model, dynamic spontaneous imbibition, Young-Laplace filling rules, Bentheimer sandstone, relative permeability
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-060.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-060)

Colour-gradient lattice Boltzmann simulations match micro-model drainage and imbibition experiments, showing dynamic imbibition follows local geometry, and reproduce Bentheimer capillary pressure and relative permeability reasonably well.

The paper validates a colour-gradient lattice Boltzmann (LB) code against PMMA micro-model experiments (decane/air, 50 µm etch depth) in single pore junctions, including a Berea thin-section pore. Quasi-static drainage and imbibition follow Young-Laplace filling rules, and snap-off bubbles are captured. In dynamic spontaneous imbibition, the nearest channel fills first rather than the narrowest, so local geometry controls the filling sequence, and LB reproduces this. The code is then applied to a Bentheimer micro-CT image (512x256x256 voxels, 4.9 µm, porosity 0.234, LB permeability 4755 mD). Computed capillary pressure (Swi about 7%) and drainage/imbibition relative permeabilities agree generally with experiment. Pc at irreducible saturation is underpredicted and non-wetting relative permeability and the imbibition endpoint are overpredicted, attributed to resolution and periodic boundary conditions.

## Macro to Nano: Correlative X-Ray and Electron Microscopy for Multi-Length Scale Characterization of Geomaterials

- ID: SCA2014-061, year: 2014
- Authors: Jeff Gelb, Renata Cicha-Szot, Irena Matyasik, Grzegorz Leśniak, Sylwia Kowalska, Maja Mroczkowska-Szerszeń, Małgorzata Kowalska-Włodarczyk, Eddy Hill, Ben Tordoff, Ken G. Lagarec, Dirk Schumann, Michael W. Phaneuf, Mike Marsh, Andy Steinbach, Carl Zeiss X-ray Microscopy
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: X-ray microscopy, FIB-SEM tomography, SEM-EDS mineralogy, correlative microscopy, mudrock nanopores, digital rock physics
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 3
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-061.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-061)

Correlates X-ray microscopy, FIB-SEM, SEM-EDS and bulk lab analyses on a mudstone to characterize minerals, organics and nanopores across length scales for digital rock physics.

Mudrocks are hierarchically porous, and no single technique captures a representative volume. A Pelplin Green Claystones (Wenlock) mudstone sample was analyzed with XRD, FTIR, ICP, mercury porosimetry and argon/nitrogen adsorption, plus XRM at 5 µm, 2 µm and 700 nm voxels, FIB-SEM tomography at 20 nm, and SEM-EDS mineral mapping at ~1 µm. XRD gave 23.7% quartz, 11.5% carbonates and 50.1% clay; XRM showed ~11% higher-Z minerals and located them in 3D. XRM guided selection of a FIB-SEM volume (65×25×22.5 µm) rich in organics and pyrite framboids, where most pores were below 100 nm, consistent with gas adsorption pore size distributions. Large-area SEM gave TOC of 2.4% versus 1.8% from FIB-SEM, and SEM-EDS gave 74% local clay versus 50% by XRD, reflecting local variation in an organic-rich area.

## Simulation of Counter-current Spontaneous Imbibition in a Simple Porous Medium Using Lattice-Boltzmann

- ID: SCA2014-062, year: 2014
- Authors: Rasoul Arabjamaloei, Douglas Ruth
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: lattice Boltzmann, Shan-Chen model, counter-current spontaneous imbibition, bubble snap-off, Carnahan-Starling equation of state, contact angle
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-062.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-062)

A single-component multiphase Shan-Chen lattice Boltzmann model reproduces the imbibition front of Unsal et al.'s glass-and-rod experiments but fails to capture capillary-instability snap-off or dead-end pressure behavior.

The study uses a 3D (D3Q15) single-component multiphase Shan-Chen lattice Boltzmann model with a Carnahan-Starling equation of state to simulate counter-current spontaneous imbibition in the glass-and-rod model of Unsal et al. The model was tested stepwise: phase separation (density ratio limited to about 50 versus about 800 for air-water because of divergence), contact angle control through solid-fluid interaction strength, and capillary rise with gravity. Bubble snap-off never occurred from capillary instability alone and was only obtained by adding a buoyancy force. The final 60×80×2000 lattice reproduced the front shape, but the dead-end vapor pressure rose monotonically instead of dropping during bubble formation and jumping at snap-off as observed experimentally. The authors attribute this to the Shan-Chen model's inability to simulate capillary instability.

## Analysis of Pore Structure of Longmaxi Shale Using the Mercury Intrusion Porosimetry Technique

- ID: SCA2014-075, year: 2014
- Authors: Botao Lin, Zheng Jiang, Yao Chen, Mian Chen, Yan Jin, Bing Hou
- Method: laboratory experiment
- Keywords: mercury intrusion porosimetry, Longmaxi shale, pore size distribution, mesopores, hydration shrinkage, sample grain size
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2014/SCA2014-075.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2014-075)

MIP on Longmaxi shale at varied grain sizes and hydration states shows 3-11 nm mesopores dominate structure, macropores dominate volume, and powdering distorts results.

The study characterizes the pore structure of outcrop Longmaxi shale (TOC 2.5-3.5%, clay 15.7%, mostly illite) with mercury intrusion porosimetry. Samples were prepared as powder (<1 mm) and flakes of 1.6, 5.1 and 7.8 mm average diameter, plus one hydrated and oven-dried sample, with SEM and CT as supplements. Pore structure is dominated by 3-11 nm mesopores (peaks at 3, 4, 7, 11 nm), while total pore volume is mostly from macropores larger than 74 µm. Grain size has little effect on the curves except for the pulverized sample, which showed suppressed peaks but the highest porosity (4.2%); 2-8 mm grains are recommended. Hydration followed by oven-drying raised cumulative volume about 1.5 times and porosity 35% (2.3 to 3.1%), attributed to clay and organic matter shrinkage creating 3-4 nm pores. SEM and CT at the resolution used (13.3 µm voxels) could not resolve these features.

## Multi-Scale and Upscaling of Digital Rock Physics with a Machine That Can Learn About Rocks

- ID: SCA2015-026, year: 2015
- Authors: R. Sungkorn, A. Morcote, G. Carpio, G. Davalos, Y. Mu, A. Grader, N. Derzhi, J. Toelke
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: digital rock physics, machine learning, rock fabric segmentation, multi-scale upscaling, Haralick texture features, Eagle Ford
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 3
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2015/SCA2015-026.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2015-026)

Presents a machine-learning framework that segments resolved and unresolved rock fabrics in large-scale images, selects optimal high-resolution sub-sample locations, and upscales properties such as porosity recursively.

Digital rock images cover under 1% of a sample volume, so representativeness across scales is a problem. The authors propose a multi-scale DRP workflow using unsupervised machine learning on rock fabrics. Keypoints are detected by wavelet decomposition and Laplacian of Gaussian, Haralick texture features (contrast, homogeneity, entropy, variance) are computed, and keypoints are clustered to segment the overview image. An optimization algorithm picks the number and locations of higher-resolution areas, and per-fabric correlations between overview intensity and high-resolution porosity upscale porosity in 2D, demonstrated on an Eagle Ford sample. The procedure can be repeated recursively until all fabrics are resolved, and a GPU implementation processes a 2000 x 3000 pixel image in about 100 seconds.

## Simulating in-Situ Conditions for Digital Core Analysis

- ID: SCA2015-032, year: 2015
- Authors: S. Linden, T. Cvjetkovic, E. Glatt, J.-O. Schwarz, A. Wiegmann
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: digital rock physics, numerical compression, in-situ stress, Berea sandstone, pore morphology method, formation factor
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2015/SCA2015-032.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2015-032)

A workflow numerically compresses an ambient-condition Berea CT image to simulate in-situ stress, showing significant changes in porosity, permeability, formation factor and, less so, two-phase properties.

Digital rock analysis normally uses ambient-condition images that ignore pore-space expansion after core uplift. The authors add a numerical uniaxial compression step (FeelMath solver) to a segmented 720x720x1024 voxel Berea sandstone image at 0.74 µm resolution, then compute single- and two-phase properties in GeoDict at pressures up to 1.43 GPa. Porosity falls linearly from 18.4% to 15.7%, absolute permeability from 108 to 66 mD, and formation factor rises from 27 to 39. Capillary pressure, pore size distribution and wetting-phase relative permeability change little, while non-wetting relative permeability declines earlier and the Archie saturation exponent drops from 2.38 to 2.04. Each simulation takes hours on 16 cores, and the next step is comparison with laboratory in-situ measurements.

## On the Measurement of Pore Geometry: A Comprehensive Petrophysical Study of Conventional Rocks

- ID: SCA2015-038, year: 2015
- Authors: Babak Salimifard, Douglas W. Ruth, Derrick Green, Brent Nassichuk
- Method: laboratory experiment
- Keywords: mercury porosimetry, NMR T2 relaxation, centrifuge capillary pressure, representative elemental volume, permeability prediction, effective relaxivity
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2015/SCA2015-038.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2015-038)

Combining MP, NMR and centrifuge data on 11 plugs, NMR-derived capillary pressure in an REV model improves permeability prediction for heterogeneous samples versus MP.

The paper tests an REV permeability model that uses pore size distribution and porosity, previously validated on 25 Ghana samples with under 35% mean error. Mercury porosimetry, 2 MHz NMR, centrifuge drainage and flow tests were run on 11 plugs of sandstone, limestone, dolomite and chalk. Coupling centrifuge with NMR gave capillary pressure curves that matched MP for homogeneous samples but differed for heterogeneous ones (E and J); dividing plugs into four segments quantified heterogeneity. NMR T2 distributions were converted to capillary pressure using effective relaxivities fitted to MP, improving prediction error from 245% to 132% (excluding chalk sample E). Sample E, a compressible chalk, was poorly predicted because NMR is measured without overburden; relaxivity calibration remains necessary.

## Two Scale Pore Analysis Applied to Low Permeability Sandstone

- ID: SCA2015-048, year: 2015
- Authors: C. A. Davy, P.M. Adler, Yang Song, L. Jeannin, G. Hauss, Thang Nguyen Kim
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: tight gas sandstone, FIB/SEM, micro-CT, fissure network permeability, mercury intrusion porosimetry, Lattice Boltzmann
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2015/SCA2015-048.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2015-048)

Combines 2D SEM fissure network statistics with micro-CT or FIB/SEM single-fissure transmissivity to predict tight sandstone permeability; micrometric fissures from micro-CT match measured gas permeability.

The study seeks the pore scales controlling flow in a tight gas sandstone (T-2390-82, porosity 2.8-3.2%, gas permeability about 21 µD at low confining stress). Characterization used MIP (peak at 350 nm), 2D SEM fissure trace maps, FIB/SEM (15 nm voxels) and micro-CT at 13, 4.4 and 0.6 µm voxel sizes. Micro-CT showed no connected network at 13 µm and barely any at 4.4 µm; only at 0.6 µm was an individual fissure (about 4 µm opening, porosity 1.34%) connected. Lattice Boltzmann computed single-fissure transmissivity, which was upscaled using the 2D SEM network density in a percolation-based permeability formula. FIB/SEM fissures (about 100 nm, porosity 1.27%) gave 3-62 nD, three to four orders below measurement, while micro-CT fissures gave 6-740 µD, consistent with centimetric plug gas permeability.

## A Multi-Scale Dynamic Predictive Model for Drainage and Imbibition Capillary Pressure in Heterogeneous Rocks

- ID: SCA2015-054, year: 2015
- Authors: X. Nie, C. Baldwin, Y. Mu, A. Grader, J. Toelke
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: lattice-Boltzmann simulation, multi-scale imaging, Darcy region, digital porous plate, capillary pressure, imbibition residual oil
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 2
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2015/SCA2015-054.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2015-054)

A dynamic multi-scale lattice-Boltzmann model with Darcy regions predicts drainage and imbibition capillary pressure, matching fully resolved simulations of a layered sphere pack at 10x coarser resolution.

Imaging and simulating representative volumes of multi-scale rocks at full resolution is impractical, so the authors develop a dynamic multi-scale lattice-Boltzmann model. Coarse micro-CT volumes are segmented into pores, solids and Darcy regions with unresolved pores. The Darcy regions take Pc-Sw curves and breakthrough pressures from separate high-resolution LBM simulations, and exchange fluid with resolved pores according to local pressure and phase. Validation used a synthetic layered sphere pack (600^3 pixels at 2 µm, three pore sizes, porosity 0.4), compared with a 60^3 coarse model at 20 µm, for flow perpendicular (Case A) and parallel (Case B) to layering. Capillary pressure curves and residual oil agree well between the fully resolved and multi-scale simulations. Residual oil is higher in Case A because of a large effective pore body-to-throat ratio (7.3 to 14.6), versus about 1.5 in Case B.

## Analysis and Laboratory for Generation of

- ID: SCA2016-020, year: 2016
- Authors: Kurdistan Chawshin, Pål-Eric Øren
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: digital rock analysis, pore network modelling, in situ contact angle, microporosity, MICP, waterflood relative permeability
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 5
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2016/SCA2016-020.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2016-020)

Multiscale digital rock workflow with in situ contact angles and pore network simulation predicts multiphase transport properties for three bimodal sandstones, matching available measured end-points reasonably well.

The study combines micro-CT, BSE/QEMSCAN imaging and MICP on three poorly sorted bimodal clastic sandstone plugs to build pore networks for resolved porosity and process-based reconstructed networks for sub-resolution microporosity. Image-based lattice Boltzmann permeability agreed reasonably for plugs 2 and 3 (225 vs 210 mD; 660 vs 786 mD), but Plug 1 differed by a factor of about 3.7 owing to heterogeneity. Sensitivity to the oil-wet fraction (water-wet, mixed-wet, oil-wet) was assessed and its recovery implications examined with Buckley-Leverett analysis.

## 4D Multi-Scale Imaging of Reaction in Carbonates: The Impact of Heterogeneity on Dissolution Regimes at the Core and Pore Scales with Applications to CO2 Storage

- ID: SCA2016-030, year: 2016
- Authors: H.P. Menke, C.A. Reynolds, Andrew M.G
- Method: laboratory experiment
- Keywords: carbonate dissolution, 4D X-ray microtomography, CO2 storage, channelling, pore-space heterogeneity, reactive flow
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2016/SCA2016-030.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2016-030)

Dynamic 4D X-ray imaging of CO2-brine dissolution in Ketton and Portland limestones at micro and core scales shows structure-dependent channelling and heterogeneous porosity propagation along flow.

The study examines how pore-space heterogeneity and scale affect carbonate dissolution by CO2-saturated brine, relevant to CO2 storage. Ketton and Portland Basebed limestones were reacted at 50 °C and 10 MPa with pH 3.1 brine at equal Darcy velocity, using 4×10 mm micro cores imaged at 4 µm and 3.8×8 cm macro cores imaged by medical CT (250×250×500 µm) over 1.5 hours. Reacted macro cores were rescanned at 27 µm to analyse connected channels. Both rocks showed channelling at the pore scale, with Ketton permeability rising one order of magnitude and Portland over five. At core scale, porosity rose by ~0.04 in both, but permeability was unchanged in Ketton and rose two orders in Portland, whose channels broke through to the outlet. Pe·Da was <<1 and pore volumes were not limiting, so structural heterogeneity rather than fluid supply controls dissolution pathways.

## A Lattice Boltzmann Approach to Wettability and Rate Effects on Relative Permeability using Digital Core Analysis

- ID: SCA2016-040, year: 2016
- Authors: Louis K. Gbayan, Christoph H. Arns
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: lattice Boltzmann, Shan-Chen model, two relaxation time, contact angle, capillary drainage transform, Fontainebleau sandstone
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2016/SCA2016-040.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2016-040)

A Shan-Chen two-relaxation-time lattice Boltzmann model is applied to reconstructed Fontainebleau sandstone, showing that initial fluid distributions strongly control the evolution of two-phase fluid configurations.

The paper addresses two-phase lattice Boltzmann simulation of fluid distributions and wettability in digital rock, where relative permeability is usually computed on imaged fluid partitions. A Shan-Chen LBM with a two-relaxation-time scheme was applied to reconstructed Fontainebleau sandstone at 3.66 and 1.83 µm resolution. Initial saturations (Sw about 53%) were set using the Euclidean distance transform, covering radius transform and capillary drainage transform, with contact angles of 76.8° and 35.0°. Fluid configurations after 20,000 iterations stayed close to the initial state for CDT and CRT, while EDT was less stable and drifted toward the CRT result. Contact-angle curvatures establish quickly, but global equilibrium is slow, needing more than 80,000 iterations, and a further coarsening of resolution gave significantly divergent results.

## Inaccessible Porosity – Insight to Pore Development and Solutions to MICP Data Interpretation

- ID: SCA2016-048, year: 2016
- Authors: Leśniak G, Such P, Komorowska K
- Method: laboratory experiment
- Keywords: MICP, inaccessible porosity, shale, helium pycnometry, nitrogen adsorption, organic matter pores
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2016/SCA2016-048.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2016-048)

Some Polish Silurian shales show MICP porosity exceeding helium total porosity; SEM and porosity data indicate isolated pores in organic matter and framboidal pyrite collapsing under high mercury pressure.

In some Silurian shales, MICP-calculated porosity exceeds helium porosity measured on powdered samples, with a sharp rise in the cumulative curve below 10 nm. More than 2000 Polish shale samples were studied, and six were examined in detail using helium pycnometry on granulated and powdered material, MICP, nitrogen adsorption, XRD, thin sections and SEM. Sample 2 had 10.22% MICP porosity against 4.62% total and 0.70% open porosity. SEM showed isolated pores in dispersed organic matter (up to ~300 nm) and in pyrite framboids. The authors conclude these closed pores, inaccessible even to helium, are opened by collapse above ~50,000 psi, so the sub-10 nm MICP peak does not reflect true pore sizes and isolated nanopores can add up to 4% of MICP porosity.

## Fractal Dimension: An Indicator to Characterize the Microstructure of Shale and Tight Gas Sands Considering Distinct Techniques and Phenomena

- ID: SCA2016-069, year: 2016
- Authors: Mayka Schmitta, Matthias Halischb, Celso P. Fernandesa, Viviane S. S. dos Santosc, Andreas Wellerd
- Method: data analysis or correlation
- Keywords: fractal dimension, mercury intrusion capillary pressure, nitrogen gas adsorption, FHH method, X-ray nano-CT, box-counting
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2016/SCA2016-069.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2016-069)

Fractal dimensions of a Brazilian shale and tight gas sandstone from MICP, N2 adsorption and X-ray CT differ by technique, reflecting the pore ranges each probes.

The study characterizes pore-space complexity of one shale (Shale-17) and one tight gas sandstone (TGS-59) from Brazilian unconventional reservoirs using fractal dimension. Fractal dimensions were derived from MICP (power-law capillary pressure relation), N2 adsorption/desorption (FHH equation) and 3D box-counting on X-ray micro- and nano-CT images. MICP and N2GA curves gave three regimes with D3<D2<D1; for Shale-17 the total fractal dimension was 2.77–2.93 (MICP) versus about 2.72–2.73 (N2GA), and for TGS-59 2.45–2.89 versus about 2.5. Mercury entrapment after extrusion raises MICP values. CT-derived fractal dimensions were lower (e.g., about 1.4–2.65) and closer to surface fractal values (D3), because image size and voxel size limit the range of pore scales captured. Ganglia (small voxel-count pores) showed very low values, and porosity increase did not correlate with fractal dimension in 3D.

## On the Influence of Mixed Wettability on the Simulation of Multi-Phase Transport Properties of Core Samples

- ID: SCA2016-081, year: 2016
- Authors: J.-O. Schwarz, S. Linden, J. Becker, C. Wagner, A. Wiegmann
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: mixed wettability, pore morphology method, relative permeability simulation, digital rock physics, Berea sandstone, contact angle
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2016/SCA2016-081.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2016-081)

Pore morphology simulations on a segmented Berea sandstone XCT image show mineral-specific contact angles shift the relative permeability crossover from Sw 0.65 to 0.57 versus a constant angle.

Pore-scale prediction of relative permeability usually assumes a single contact angle, which ignores mineral-specific wettability. The authors implemented a multiple-contact-angle pore morphology method in GeoDict and applied it to a synchrotron XCT image of Berea sandstone segmented into quartz, feldspar, ankerite and zircon. Primary drainage was simulated with a constant 10° angle and with generic mineral-specific angles (10–70°), followed by Stokes flow for relative permeabilities, with Corey fits covering saturation ranges where no quasi-stationary states exist. Multiple angles gave lower capillary pressures, lower irreducible water saturation (~10% vs ~16%) and lower displacement pressure (12 vs 14 kPa). The kr,w/kr,o crossover moved from Sw=0.65 (constant angle) to 0.57 (multiple angles), so ignoring mixed wettability may overestimate the crossover saturation.

## Coupling Lattice Boltzmann and Finite Volume Methods for Simulation of Reactive Transport

- ID: SCA2016-086, year: 2016
- Authors: Min Liu, Peyman Mostaghimi
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: lattice Boltzmann, finite volume, reactive transport, carbonate dissolution, micro-CT, critical radius
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2016/SCA2016-086.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2016-086)

A coupled lattice Boltzmann and finite volume pore-scale model reproduces dynamic micro-CT imaging of Ketton carbonate dissolution, matching porosity, permeability-porosity trends and pore structure changes.

The paper develops a 3D pore-scale reactive transport model that couples a D3Q19 lattice Boltzmann flow solver with a finite volume advection-diffusion and first-order surface reaction scheme, run directly on micro-CT images. It is validated against published dynamic imaging of Ketton carbonate dissolution at Pe=2100 and Da=0.05, with no tuning parameters. Simulated dissolution is uniform like the experiment, and porosity profiles differ by 3.8% on average. The permeability-porosity power-law exponent is 4.83 for simulation versus 4.58 for experiment (5.4% difference). Chord length distributions and normalized critical radius match experiment, with critical radius differing by under 6%.

## Primary Drainage Relative Permeability in Hydrocarbon Reservoirs Interpreted Using Digital Rock Physics

- ID: SCA2017-006, year: 2017
- Authors: Sven Roth, Hu Dong, Martin J. Blunt, Zubair Kalam, Djelloul Hammadi
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: digital rock physics, multi-scale imaging, pore network model upscaling, primary drainage relative permeability, carbonate Dunham classification, waterflood wettability
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2017/SCA2017-006.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2017-006)

Multi-scale imaging and upscaled pore-network modelling of 54 carbonate samples links Dunham rock type to primary drainage and waterflood relative permeability, with opposite trends.

The study uses digital rock physics to predict relative permeability for 54 samples from three zones of a Lower Cretaceous carbonate reservoir. Plug X-ray CT, micro-CT, nano-CT (65 nm voxels), BSE mosaics and QEMSCAN feed a three-stage upscaling of pore networks from micro- to macro- to vuggy porosity. Digital porosity and permeability agree with laboratory values within about 2 porosity units and a factor of two. Primary drainage cross-over points of the relative permeability curves separate mud-dominated rocks (Sw 0.4–0.7, krel 0.05–0.2) from grain-dominated rocks (Sw 0.57–0.84, krel 0.04–0.33). Grain-dominated rocks tend to be mixed to oil-wet and show early water breakthrough and poor displacement efficiency in waterflooding, while mud-dominated rocks are mixed to water-wet with more favourable recovery. Subdividing by pore system gave no advantage over Dunham classification, and the grain-dominated variability is too large for improved rock typing.

## Using Digital Rock Technology to Quality Control and Reduce Uncertainty in Relative Permeability Measurements

- ID: SCA2017-014, year: 2017
- Authors: Josephina Schembre-McCabe, Jairam Kamath
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: digital rock, pore network model, imbibition relative permeability, micro-CT segmentation, wettability, quality control
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 2
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2017/SCA2017-014.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2017-014)

Pore network models on micro-CT images bound water-wet and oil-wet imbibition kr, flagging outlier unsteady-state measurements and narrowing remaining oil after 1 PVI from 30-60% to 42-58% OIP.

Unsteady-state water-displacing-oil relative permeability data from three wells over seven years showed large scatter, with remaining oil after 1 PVI ranging from 30 to 65% OIP. Six samples in three groups of similar porosity, permeability and pore throat size were imaged by micro-CT (0.7 µm voxel, 3 mm plugs), with three-phase segmentation guided by porosity and clay from other measurements. Pore network models reproduced measured permeability, drainage capillary pressure and gas-oil centrifuge kr, building confidence in the networks. Water-wet (30-70°) and oil-wet (120-150°) imbibition kr calculations bracketed expected behavior; samples falling outside this envelope or inconsistent with Amott/USBM wettability data were flagged as outliers (e.g., S1.2, S3.2). Removing the outliers cut the range of remaining oil after 1 PVI to 42-58% OIP.

## Investigation of Salt-Bearing Sediments Through Digital Rock Technology Together with Experimental Core Analysis

- ID: SCA2017-015, year: 2017
- Authors: Marisa B. Rydzy, Ben Anger, Stefan Hertel, Jesse Dietderich, Jorge Patino, Matthias Appel
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: halite cement, micro X-ray CT, MRI core flood, salt dissolution, lattice-Boltzmann permeability, pore size distribution
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2017/SCA2017-015.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2017-015)

Combines micro-CT digital rock analysis, helium porosity, and MRI-monitored salt-dissolution floods to show halite fills larger pores and cuts permeability and porosity, with scale-dependent heterogeneity.

The study examines how halite saturation and distribution control porosity and permeability in halite-cemented sandstone. Sixteen 0.6 cm mini plugs were imaged by MXCT (~2 µm), segmented, and used for pore/grain size analysis and Lattice-Boltzmann permeability, alongside helium porosity on 2.54 cm plugs. Halite occurred in layers, with pores either fully filled or free, and preferentially in larger pores (paleo-pore sizes 40-80 µm vs 30-60 µm for halite-free), linked to larger grain size. Permeability fell roughly exponentially with salt saturation; measured values rose from 4-20 mD (salt-filled) to 600-800 mD after dissolution, while calculated porosity differed from measured by up to 6 p.u. MRI-monitored low-salinity floods revealed stratification hidden by salt, so mini-plug results may represent only one layer, and the dissolution front cut across layers.

## Prediction of Permeability from Euler Characteristic of 3D Images

- ID: SCA2017-016, year: 2017
- Authors: Z. Liu, A. Herring, V. Robins, R. T. Armstrong
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: Euler characteristic, permeability prediction, Lattice Boltzmann, persistent homology, Betti numbers, void ratio
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2017/SCA2017-016.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2017-016)

Lattice Boltzmann simulations on stochastic 2D and 3D models test Scholz's Euler-characteristic permeability model; adding void ratio makes it work in 3D.

The paper tests whether permeability can be predicted from the Euler characteristic and grain number, as proposed by Scholz et al. for 2D media, and extends this to 3D. Quasi-2D models (round, ellipse, square grains) and 3D models (spheres, sticks, and eroded/dilated micro-CT Robuglass) were generated, and single-phase flow was simulated with a D3Q19 Lattice-Boltzmann code to get permeability. In q2D, the data collapse onto one curve when the input grain number is used. Measured grain numbers (weighted or persistent-homology) match the input only at low grain density, and the collapse degrades as grains overlap. In 3D, β1/Np does not give a single trend, but multiplying by the void ratio φ/(1−φ) gives similar scaling slopes across all three models.

## How to use Infrared Spectroscopy Mineralogy Data to Calibrate Logs

- ID: SCA2017-067, year: 2017
- Authors: Adam K Moss, Gavin Hunt
- Method: data analysis or correlation
- Keywords: SWIR infrared spectroscopy, core mineralogy, gamma ray shale volume, density log porosity, clay bound water T2 cut-off, mini-permeability
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 2
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2017/SCA2017-067.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2017-067)

Shows how SWIR infrared core mineralogy calibrates gamma shale volume, density porosity and NMR clay-bound-water cut-offs, and reveals mineralogical controls on permeability, using a North Sea well.

High-resolution SWIR hyperspectral mineralogy from 64.5 m of core in North Sea well 21/16-2 (Fulmar and Zechstein formations) was used to calibrate log interpretation models. Clay volumes from the scanner were used with an optimiser to derive depth-varying gamma minimum and maximum values, showing the operator's single values (35 and 115 API) were inadequate.
