Mini-permeability comparison showed clay reducing permeability at 7099-7108 ft and dolomite controlling it in the Zechstein, with a weak link to the hydrocarbon spectral signal.

## Linking Mineral Reactive Surface Area with

- ID: SCA2017-076, year: 2017
- Method: measurement technique or apparatus
- Keywords: specific surface area, BET nitrogen adsorption, stereology, SEM-EDS mineral mapping, mercury porosimetry, reactive surface area
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2017/SCA2017-076.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2017-076)

Combines BET, porosimetry, XRF/XRD, SEM-EDS and micro-CT with stereology to estimate accessible reactive surface area of individual minerals in a geothermal sandstone, calibrated to bulk BET.

Reactive transport modelling of fluid-rock reactions in geothermal and CCUS reservoirs needs the accessible surface area of individual minerals, which is hard to measure in heterogeneous rock. The authors studied sandstone from the Vydmantai-1 geothermal well in Lithuania, measuring porosity by helium pycnometry (21.9 ± 0.4%), pore size distribution by Hg porosimetry, and bulk SSA by N2 BET (1.447 m2/g). Mineralogy came from XRF, XRD and SEM-EDS, and was mapped onto SEM-BSE (1.2 µm) and SEM-EDS (2.4 µm) images, with 3D micro-CT (9.5 µm) used for pore size comparison. Stereological SSA from images was corrected for resolution and for roughness using literature BET values of pure minerals, giving a bulk accessible SSA of about 1.441 m2/g that agrees with BET. Image-derived mineral fractions agreed with XRD+XRF, except for kaolinite, and PSD peaks differed between Hg porosimetry (15 µm) and SEM (30 µm). Resolution correction alone is suggested to give a lower bound of accessible area and roughness correction an upper bound.

## Combining porosimetry and Purcell permeability modeling to calibrate FZI and define a dynamic permeability cut off

- ID: SCA2017-081, year: 2017
- Authors: Philippe Rabiller
- Method: data analysis or correlation
- Keywords: MICP, Purcell permeability model, flow zone indicator, PHIZ, mean hydraulic radius, permeability cut-off
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2017/SCA2017-081.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2017-081)

A Purcell-PSKT workflow on MICP data derives PHIZ, true RQI and FZI, calibrates routine core data, and defines a dynamic permeability cut-off.

FZI assumes porosity is effective for flow, but in complex carbonates only a variable fraction of total porosity (PHIZ) is reached by the large pore throats that control permeability. The paper proposes a Purcell-PSKT model applied to MICP pore throat size distributions, using the Katz-Thompson, Pittman and Walls-Amaefule trial solutions to pick the lower throat limit. This gives permeability, PHIZ and true RQI/FZI, which predict permeability better than the classic Purcell model. Validation uses the Amaefule MHR/PHI versus FZI plot: with PHIZ the points align on the diagonal, while with total porosity and RCA FZI they form a cloud. RCA poro-perm (481 points, 7 wells) is calibrated to MICP-derived RQI, and MHR predicted by k-NN is converted to a field Pc to give a dynamic permeability cut-off; the workflow has been applied to over 4000 MICP measurements.

## Prediction of Relative Permeability and Capillary Pressure using Digital Rock Physics: Case Study on two Giant Middle Eastern Carbonate Reservoirs

- ID: SCA2017-083, year: 2017
- Authors: Kamel Zahaf, Thierry F. Lecoq, Badar S. AL Badi, Sven Roth, Hu Dong, Martin J. Blunt
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: digital rock physics, pore network modeling, waterflood relative permeability, carbonate wettability, multi-scale upscaling, MICP calibration
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2017/SCA2017-083.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2017-083)

Multi-scale imaging and generalized network modeling of 16 Middle Eastern carbonates predict capillary pressure and relative permeability; limestones show oil-wet, poor-recovery waterflood behavior, dolomites mixed-wet and more favorable.

Carbonate heterogeneity limits what SCAL can resolve, so the authors applied a digital rock workflow to 16 limestone and dolomite samples from two Middle Eastern fields. Workflow combined micro-CT, QEMSCAN, BSE mosaics and nano-CT subsampling with multi-scale generalized network modeling and upscaling, validated against porosity, permeability and MICP. Contact angles were tuned to one waterflood capillary pressure curve (about 60% oil-wet pore space, mean angle 135°), and predicted waterflood relative permeability matched measured oil relative permeability on benchmark samples, though water end points did not match. Limestones with micro, macro and vuggy porosity had cross-over saturation below 0.5, high water relative permeability and strong hysteresis, implying poor recovery. Dolomites with narrower pore size ranges had cross-over saturations above 0.5 and low water relative permeability, indicating mixed-wet behavior and better recovery.

## A Robust Integration Between Geochemistry and Advanced Digital Core Analysis Methods to Generate High-Resolution Porosity-Permeability Logs: A Case Study on a Major Unconventional Play in Middle East

- ID: SCA2017-090, year: 2017
- Authors: Adnan Al-Shamali, Naveen K. Verma, Osama Al Jallad, Avrami Grader, Joel Walls, Safouh Kornfol, Anyela Morcote
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: FIB-SEM digital rock, Lattice Boltzmann permeability, dual-energy CT, Rock-Eval pyrolysis, organic porosity, Najmah Formation
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2017/SCA2017-090.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2017-090)

Combining dual-energy CT, SEM/FIB-SEM digital rock and geochemistry, the study corrects porosity and permeability of Kuwait's Najmah shale, raising permeability 5-20 times.

Conventional logs over-estimate porosity in mature, organic-rich Najmah Formation source rock because non-movable oil and kerogen are read as movable fluid. Sixty feet of core were scanned with dual-energy CT and spectral gamma, and 28 plugs were analyzed with XRD, LECO TOC, Rock-Eval, MICP, 2D SEM and 3D FIB-SEM at 5-15 nm. Most porosity was organic-hosted, SEM effective porosity averaged 5%, and Lattice Boltzmann permeability ranged from 41 to 20017 nD. Adding clay-bound porosity (1.4%) and the S1+0.4*S2 hydrocarbon volume roughly doubled porosity to about 10% in the upper Najmah, and the porosity log matched wireline density porosity better. Eroding the producible organic matter from the 3D volumes and recomputing permeability gave a new poroperm trend, with permeability increasing by 5X to 20X.

## Uncertainty Quantification in Image Segmentation for Image-Based Rock Physics in a Shaly-Sandstone

- ID: SCA2018-034, year: 2018
- Authors: James Howard, Sam Lin, Shawn Zhang
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: image segmentation, machine learning segmentation, microCT, uncertainty quantification, digital rock physics, chlorite clay microporosity
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2018/SCA2018-034.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2018-034)

Compares threshold and machine-learning segmentation of microCT images of chlorite-bearing sandstone, quantifying porosity uncertainty: over 100% for thresholding versus about 30% or less for ML.

Image segmentation in image-based rock physics is subjective, and its uncertainty is rarely quantified. Four shaly-sandstone samples with pore-lining chlorite/smectite were imaged by microCT at 4 and 2 microns and segmented by random-forest ML by two operators and by global thresholding. Properties (porosity, pore size, permeability, formation factor, MICP) were simulated and compared with core, NMR and MICP data. ML-segmented porosity for Sample B ranged 0.103-0.132 depending on seeding, versus core porosity of 0.175, and cementation exponents were 1.9-2.5. Threshold segmentation matched core porosity but misrepresented pore distribution, with uncertainty over 100%, while ML limited it to roughly 30% or lower. Calculated MICP matched measurement only for the high-permeability sample because sub-resolution clay microporosity was not resolved.

## Non-Destructive Pore-Scale Approach to Evaluate Elastic Properties of Shale Samples by Imaging, Modeling and Simulation

- ID: SCA2018-037, year: 2018
- Authors: Weifeng Lv, Langqiu Flora Sun
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: digital rock physics, shale elastic moduli, finite element method, QEMScan mineral map, multi-phase segmentation, representative size
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2018/SCA2018-037.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2018-037)

Virtual compression tests by FEM on a multi-phase SEM/QEMScan mineral map of a brittle shale give Young's modulus ~62 GPa and Poisson's ratio ~0.2, with Poisson's ratio matching logs.

Brittle shale cores often break before stress-strain tests, so elastic moduli are uncertain. The authors segment a 2D QEMScan mineral map (1971×1977 µm, 1 µm/pixel) of an Upper Ordovician black shale from Sichuan Basin into nine minerals plus pores. They run FEM (Garboczi scheme, energy minimization) on a 600×600 µm area with two stretching directions. Calculated Young's moduli are 62.3 and 62.5 GPa and Poisson's ratios 0.200 and 0.195, indicating isotropy. Poisson's ratio agrees with logs, but Young's modulus is systematically higher than the log value (~40 GPa). Kerogen volume from BSE images stabilizes at ~2.2% above 170² µm² FOV, defining a representative size. The mineral map underestimates porosity and kerogen, and the study is limited to 2D.

## A Methodology to Predict the Gas Permeability Parameters of Tight Reservoirs from Nitrogen Sorption Isotherms and Mercury Porosimetry Curves

- ID: SCA2018-046, year: 2018
- Authors: Christos D. Tsakiroglou, Adnan Al Hinai, Reza Rezaee
- Method: theory or analytical model
- Keywords: percolation theory, N2 sorption isotherms, mercury intrusion, Knudsen diffusion, critical path analysis, shale permeability
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2018/SCA2018-046.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2018-046)

Percolation-based inverse modeling of N2 sorption and Hg intrusion gives shale pore-network parameters, from which permeability and Knudsen diffusion are predicted; diffusion is predicted well, permeability only at low Kn.

The paper develops analytic percolation-theory models of N2 adsorption/desorption isotherms and Hg intrusion for a pore-and-throat network with bimodal pore and throat size distributions, shape factors and pore accessibility functions. Parameters are estimated stepwise by inverse modeling of N2 adsorption, N2 desorption and Hg intrusion data for Perth Basin shale samples AC1, AC5 and AC8. Critical path analysis then gives liquid permeability and Knudsen diffusion coefficient from the fitted parameters. The Knudsen diffusion coefficient was predicted satisfactorily for all samples, e.g. AC5 predicted 6.7e-9 m2/s (S1) vs measured 7.7e-9. Permeability was predicted reasonably only for AC5 (36.8 and 109.4 nD vs 61 nD measured) and failed for AC1 and AC8. This is consistent with Knudsen flow dominating in AC1 and AC8 (Kn about 10 to 32), while AC5 lies in the transition regime.

## Permeability Prediction using multivariant structural regression

- ID: SCA2019-013, year: 2019
- Authors: Matthew Andrew
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: permeability prediction, machine learning regression, Extra-Trees, synthetic pore networks, digital rock, Hagen-Poiseuille scaling
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2019/SCA2019-013.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2019-013)

An Extra-Trees regression trained on synthetic polyhedral grain packings predicts permeability from 2D-extracted pore statistics, reaching <4% test error and <25% error on 36 real digital rocks.

Permeability prediction from pore-scale images normally requires costly flow simulation or inaccurate Kozeny-Carman type models. The author generated 2,190 synthetic intergranular networks from convex polyhedral grains, simulated Stokes flow on each, and extended the set to 219,000 samples by Hagen-Poiseuille scaling of voxel size (10 nm to 10 µm). A 34-component feature vector of grain, pore and throat statistics, measured on 2D slices, was regressed against log permeability with Extra-Trees; the standard deviation of inscribed throat radius was the most important feature. Test error was RMSFE <4%. Validation on 36 real sandstone and micritic carbonate images (Digital Rocks Portal and nano-CT) spanning 10 µD to 10 D gave RMSFE <25%, with prediction taking under a minute.

## A fast FFT method for 3D pore-scale rock-typing of heterogeneous rock samples via Minkowski functionals and hydraulic attributes

- ID: SCA2019-014, year: 2019
- Authors: Han Jiang, Christoph H. Arns
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: Minkowski functionals, FFT regional curvature, rock-typing, Boolean model, capillary drainage transform, Gaussian mixture classification
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2019/SCA2019-014.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2019-014)

An FFT algorithm computes regional Minkowski measures independent of support size; on layered Boolean models, adding saturation to classification smooths and biases layer boundaries versus morphology alone.

Rock-typing of heterogeneous or laminated samples from micro-CT needs regional Minkowski measures over large averaging supports, which are costly with direct summation or support-shift algorithms. The authors introduce an FFT convolution method using additivity of vertex-configuration histograms, whose cost does not depend on support size, and compare it on a 400^3 Bentheimer sub-volume. They test classification on a layered Boolean model (spheres of radius 13 and 26 voxels, porosity ~0.24 each, 1200x1000x800 voxels) using Gaussian mixture models on four Minkowski measures with and without drainage-simulated saturation (invasion radius 6.6, Sw=0.38). Mean curvature and surface area gave the best layer separation, and Minkowski measures alone recovered layer thicknesses almost exactly (e.g. 168/298/300/173 vs 170/300/300/170 voxels with b=30). Adding saturation increased boundary standard deviation and biased the interface toward the larger-pore rock type (e.g. layer 2 of 367 vs 300 voxels).

## Digital core repository coupled with machine learning as a tool to classify and assess petrophysical rock properties

- ID: SCA2019-025, year: 2019
- Authors: Vanessa Hébert, Thierry Porcher, Valentin Planes, Marie Léger, Anna Alperovich, Bastian Goldluecke, Olivier Rodriguez, Souhail Youssef
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: convolutional neural network, digital rock physics, micro-CT segmentation, porosity prediction, transfer learning, rock type classification
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 4
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2019/SCA2019-025.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2019-025)

CNNs trained on micro-CT images of four reservoir rocks classify rock type with 100% accuracy and estimate Berea porosity directly from grayscale volumes, with under-prediction of about 3%.

The paper explores deep learning on micro-CT images of four reservoir analogue rocks (Estaillades, Savonnieres, Berea, Fontainebleau) at 3-4 µm voxel size, split into 100^3 voxel sub-blocks. A transfer-learned Inception-V3 network (RockClass), trained on 4,000 2D slices, classified 1,200 test slices with 100% accuracy for both lithology and formation. A 3D regression CNN (RegPhi) predicted Berea porosity from grayscale blocks without segmentation, with median relative error under 15% and mean 18%. A 3D AutoEncoder with a porosity-guided bottleneck segmented pores automatically; segmenting a 1000^3 image takes 15-20 minutes after two days of training. Reference porosity averaged 21.5% (9-36%), versus 18.6% for RegPhi and 18.8% for AutoEncoder, an under-estimate of 2.7-2.9%.

## Modeling Permeability in Carbonate Rocks

- ID: SCA2019-034, year: 2019
- Authors: Moustafa Dernaika, Shehadeh Masalmeh, Bashar Mansour, Osama Al-Jallad, Safouh Koronfol
- Method: data analysis or correlation
- Keywords: carbonate permeability prediction, porosity-permeability trends, rock typing, flow zone indicator, Winland r35, Lucia rock fabric number
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 5
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2019/SCA2019-034.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2019-034)

Over 1000 carbonate plugs from 7 Middle East reservoirs show three texture-controlled poro-perm trends; Kozeny-Carman FZI and Lucia RFN fit reasonably, Winland r35 fits poorly.

Permeability prediction in heterogeneous carbonates is difficult, so the authors studied over 1000 plugs from 7 Middle East reservoirs with helium porosity, gas permeability, thin sections, MICP and plug CT screening. Texture (grainy, mixed, muddy) produced three distinct power-law poro-perm trends, with diagenesis controlling the extent along each trend; 25 rock types were defined from Dunham texture, diagenesis and capillary pressure. Kozeny-Carman (FZI), Lucia (RFN) and Winland (r35) models were fitted. Average FZI was 3.5, 0.9 and 0.35 for grainy, mixed and muddy textures. FZI and RFN predicted permeability within about a factor of 2, except grainy rocks with RFN (up to one order of magnitude), while samples with the same FZI or RFN did not share the same flow unit. Winland r35 trends did not follow the texture-based behavior and gave poor matches.

## Transport properties of the Cobourg Limestone: A benchmark investigation

- ID: SCA2019-037, year: 2019
- Authors: C. A. Davy, Zhazha Hu, A.P.S. Selvadurai, Jop Klaver, M.-C. Willemetz, F. Agostini, F. Skoczylas, Jan Dewanckele, Alexandra Amann-Hildenbrand, Roland Lenormand
- Method: laboratory experiment
- Keywords: Cobourg limestone, Klinkenberg correction, gas permeability, stress sensitivity, permeability anisotropy, nanoDarcy permeability
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 2
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2019/SCA2019-037.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2019-037)

Four laboratories measured porosity and Klinkenberg-corrected permeability of heterogeneous, anisotropic Cobourg limestone, finding values spanning 0.1 nD to 100 D that are dominated by heterogeneity, not method.

Cobourg limestone, a candidate host rock for a Canadian nuclear waste repository, is heterogeneous and very tight, so four laboratories (McGill, RWTH Aachen, Centrale Lille, Cydarex) ran a permeability benchmark on samples from the same quarry blocks. Methods included steady-state liquid flow, helium pressure decay, quasi-static gas tests and small-sample or resin-disk gas tests with numerical interpretation, all with Klinkenberg correction. Porosity ranged from 0.33 to 2.51%. Permeability normal to bedding was around 1 nD (McGill 2-4×10^-21 m²), while flow parallel to bedding ranged from 10^-19 to 10^-16 m². Permeability fell 49-67% from 5 to 20 MPa at Aachen with strong hysteresis, consistent with slit-like pores. Variability could not be explained by measurement error (~20%), stress effects or sample size, so the rock proved too heterogeneous to serve as a reference material.

## Application of digital petrophysics to Brazilian pre-salt carbonate rock: comparative study on two different facies

- ID: SCA2019-068, year: 2019
- Authors: Titly Farhana Faisal, Rodolfo Victor, Rodrigo Surmas, Igor Bondino
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: digital rock physics, micro-CT, pre-salt carbonates, elastic properties simulation, image segmentation, pore network extraction
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2019/SCA2019-068.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2019-068)

Digital rock workflow applied to two Brazilian pre-salt carbonates (coquina and spherulite) compares predicted elastic and flow properties with lab data, showing overestimation and segmentation sensitivity.

The paper uses digital rock physics to compare variability in two Brazilian pre-salt carbonate facies. Sample S1 is a coquina with 754 mD permeability and 14.1% porosity, mainly calcite and quartz. Sample S2 is a spherulite with 6.15 mD permeability and 13.4% porosity. Both were imaged by micro-CT at three resolutions, including a full plug at coarse resolution, and used to compute elastic properties, single-phase flow and pore network properties. Numerical predictions overestimated laboratory measurements in all cases, and the authors compare the degree of overestimation between samples and discuss how segmentation choices affect derived properties.

## Slip-flow in tight carbonate for permeability estimation using 3D digital pore space

- ID: SCA2019-078, year: 2019
- Authors: Krakowska P, Madejski P, Puskarczyk E, Habrat M
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: slip flow, digital rock, micro-CT, tight carbonate, pulse-decay permeability, CFD
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2019/SCA2019-078.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2019-078)

CFD slip-flow simulation on a micro-CT digital pore model of a tight carbonate predicts nitrogen permeability within 10% of pulse-decay measurements.

Standard continuum-flow CFD underestimates permeability in tight rocks where gas slippage matters. A tight gas carbonate sample was imaged by X-ray micro-CT at 0.8 µm voxel size, and the pore space and microfractures were characterized by many geometric parameters (volume, surface area, thickness, ellipsoid radii, Feret diameter, sphericity, flatness, elongation). Tortuosity was computed from a pore-space skeleton. The pore geometry was meshed for finite-volume simulation of nitrogen flow with a slip-flow approach, yielding velocity and mass flow rate fields. The resulting permeability agreed with pulse-decay measurements on the core within 10%.

## Digital Rock Model based on nCT images as an input to Deep Learning for permeability simulation

- ID: SCA2019-081, year: 2019
- Authors: Edyta Puskarczyk, Paulina I. Krakowska, Magdalena Habrat, Paweł Madejski, Mariusz Jędrychowski
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: nano-computed tomography, tortuosity, artificial neural network, permeability prediction, tight rocks, poROSE
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2019/SCA2019-081.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2019-081)

Uses nCT-derived pore parameters, including tortuosity from modified poROSE algorithms, as inputs to neural networks to predict permeability in tight rocks, compared with lab and flow simulation.

The study addresses permeability estimation in low-porosity, low-permeability rocks (shales, tight sandstones and carbonates) using nanotomography (nCT). Images were processed to obtain porosity, pore channel number and length, connectivity, directions and tortuosity, with the authors' modified poROSE algorithm computing tortuosity by standard and novel approaches. These quantitative parameters were fed to artificial neural networks (MLP and RGB) to simulate permeability. A relation between nCT parameters and permeability was found, and the results were compared with laboratory measurements and with fluid flow simulations. The authors conclude that the new image-processing algorithms and high-resolution data improve permeability estimation and that deep learning is effective for this purpose.

## Computed X-ray tomography data in multiple linear regression analysis on tight rocks for permeability estimation

- ID: SCA2019-083, year: 2019
- Authors: Krakowska P, Puskarczyk E, Madejski P, Habrat M, Dohnalik M
- Method: data analysis or correlation
- Keywords: X-ray computed tomography, multiple linear regression, tight rock permeability, Kozeny equation, pore shape parameters
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2019/SCA2019-083.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2019-083)

Multiple linear regression on 291 CT-derived pore-shape parameters yields a permeability equation for 60 tight rocks of mixed lithology, with R=0.82 and R²=0.67.

The Kozeny relation assumes a bundle of capillary tubes and does not hold for tight sandstones and carbonates, so a non-invasive permeability estimate is needed. Sixty tight sandstone, mudstone, limestone and dolomite samples were imaged by X-ray CT, and 291 pore-space parameters were computed per sample (volume, surface area, thickness, anisotropy, flatness, Feret diameters, moments of inertia, sphericity and combinations), with statistics over all pores and for the largest pore. Multiple linear regression selected six ratio-type parameters, such as Feret breadth/volume, flatness/anisotropy and moments of inertia ratios. The resulting lithology-independent equation has a correlation coefficient of 0.82 and a determination coefficient of 0.67. Results were compared with pulse-decay and pressure-decay permeability measurements.

## Pore-scale characterization of Norwegian Continental Shelf Reservoir based on Digital Rock Simulation

- ID: SCA2019-089, year: 2019
- Authors: Fatime Zekiri, Pit Arnold, Jan Steckhan, Holger Ott
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: digital rock simulation, micro-CT, synchrotron imaging, pore network model, sandstone permeability, capillary pressure
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2019/SCA2019-089.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2019-089)

OMV's digital rock pilot simulates porosity, permeability and capillary pressure from micro-CT of five Norwegian Continental Shelf sandstone cores, showing good agreement with experiments.

OMV started a Digital Rock Simulation project within its DigitUP digitalization initiative to provide multiple pore-scale realizations of reservoir properties quickly for project decisions. The first pilot covers an offshore gas-bearing sandstone with porosity of about 20–30% and permeability above 1000 mD, with some lower-quality zones (porosity <15%, permeability <100 mD). Five cores spanning the range of rock quality were scanned at Montanuniversität Leoben using laboratory and synchrotron micro-CT. Initial simulations of porosity, permeability and capillary pressure agree well with experimental results. Future work will compare direct computation with pore network modelling, address more complex reservoirs such as carbonates, and test upscaling approaches.

## Multi-scale Digital Rock: Application of a multi-scale multi-phase workflow to a Carbonate reservoir rock

- ID: SCA2021-001, year: 2021
- Authors: Andrew Fager, Hiroshi Otomo, Rafael Salazar-Tio, Ganapathi Balasubramanian, Bernd Crouse, Raoyang Zhang, Hudong Chen, Josephina Schembre-McCabe Dassault Systèmes
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: multi-scale digital rock, lattice Boltzmann, sub-resolution porous media, carbonate imbibition, mercury intrusion simulation, confocal microscopy
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2021/SCA2021-001.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2021-001)

A multi-scale lattice Boltzmann workflow with sub-resolution porous media constitutive relationships is applied to Indiana limestone, matching permeability and imbibition and showing under-resolved pores increase oil mobilization.

Carbonates with pore sizes spanning orders of magnitude cannot be captured by one microCT resolution, so the authors extend a Shan-Chen LBM solver to treat under-resolved porous media (PM) pixels using constitutive relationships (k-porosity, Pc, Kr). Indiana limestone was imaged by microCT (3.5 µm/pixel, 600³) and confocal microscopy (0.128 µm/pixel); the image was segmented into 7% pore, 21.3% micrite PM with mean porosity 0.186. Single-phase permeability was 7.8 mD for the multi-scale model, consistent with the single-resolution (7.7 mD), registered dual-porosity (8.2 mD) and lab values (6.7-14.6 mD). A PM-permeability parametric study recovered the pore and solid end members, and an extended percolation-invasion MIP method recovered the second entry pressure down to about 0.2 µm. Multi-scale imbibition agreed with the registered model on a 100³ sub-volume and gave water saturation consistent with the 62% from lab spontaneous imbibition, with greater oil mobilization than the single-scale model; mixed-wet cases behaved as expected.

## Digital Core Analysis as an Efficient Tool for Acid Treatment Optimization

- ID: SCA2021-004, year: 2021
- Authors: Evgeny Ivanov, Dimitry Korobkov, Igor Varfolomeev, Alexander Demianov, Anna Beletskaya, Mikhail Stukan
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: acidizing, wormhole, PVBT, microCT digital rock, Austin chalk, reactive flow simulation
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2021/SCA2021-004.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2021-004)

A two-scale digital rock workflow, combining microCT pore-scale simulation and Darcy-scale reactive flow tuned to mini-plug acid floods, reproduces wormholes and PVBT in Austin chalk.

Acidizing design needs PVBT and optimum injection rate, but destructive tests on limited core make scenario comparison difficult. The authors acidized 8 mm Austin chalk mini-plugs with 15 wt% HCl (one trial core run to breakthrough, two target cores stopped early), with resistivity and pressure monitoring and before/after microCT at ~2.2 µm voxels. Pore-scale direct hydrodynamics simulations on 396³ sub-blocks gave porosity–permeability–surface area correlations, which populated a ~200×200×400 voxel Darcy-scale reactive flow model run on GPUs. After tuning on the trial core (PVBT 1.3 PV), simulated post-treatment porosity (25.6%, 27.5%) and permeability (1.1 D, 760 mD) were close to measured values (26%, 27.6%; 1.4 D, 980 mD), with qualitatively similar wormhole patterns. The model then produced PVBT-versus-injection-rate curves from 0.04 to 1 ml/min, which is not feasible experimentally; permeability deviations were attributed to neglecting Navier-Stokes flow in wormholes.

## 3D Multiclass Digital Core Models via microCT, SEM-EDS and Deep Learning

- ID: SCA2021-006, year: 2021
- Authors: Igor Varfolomeev, Vladimir Svinin, Ivan Yakimchuk
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: microCT segmentation, QEMSCAN SEM-EDS, image registration, U-net CNN, Berea sandstone, multiclass digital rock
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2021/SCA2021-006.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2021-006)

Registers QEMSCAN mineral maps to microCT of Berea miniplugs to train CNN multiclass segmentation, which outperforms Naïve Bayes and LightGBM despite image non-uniformity.

Conventional binary microCT segmentation is operator-dependent and cannot represent sub-resolution porosity or clays. The authors scanned five Ø8 mm Berea miniplugs (~4000^3 voxels, ~2.2 µm) and cut them into 45 polished surfaces mapped by QEMSCAN. Mineral maps were registered to the microCT via surface-profile matching, area-based optimization and optical-flow de-warping, then relabeled (e.g. clay mixtures, unknowns) to build ground truth. Naïve Bayes, LightGBM with LBP features, 3-slice U-net, 3D U-net and 3D U-net+GAN were compared; Otsu reached only 0.813 accuracy and 0.109 F1, and CNNs scored higher with more realistic grains. Segmentation quality depended on training set size (below ~10 slices insufficient) and degraded with distance from the microCT optical axis, indicating intrinsic non-uniformity rather than bad slices.

## The Problem of Stability of Gas-Condensate Mixture at Pore-Scale: The Study by Density Functional Hydrodynamics

- ID: SCA2021-009, year: 2021
- Authors: Oleg Dinariev, Nikolay Evseev, Denis Klemin
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: density functional hydrodynamics, gas-condensate, pseudocomponent lumping, digital rock, relative permeability, Helmholtz energy
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2021/SCA2021-009.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2021-009)

Density functional hydrodynamics with a new lumping technique simulates gas-condensate flow on a micro-CT carbonate model; reaching thermodynamic equilibrium narrows the two-phase mobility region.

Laboratory gas-condensate experiments may not reach thermodynamic equilibrium, unlike reservoir conditions far from wells. The authors develop a space-reduction lumping technique compatible with density functional hydrodynamics (DFH), reducing an 11-component rich gas-condensate mixture to 4 pseudocomponents with at most 5% error. They simulate flow in a micro-CT digital model of a low-permeability carbonate (0.82 µm/voxel, 500³ voxels, porosity 0.175, 1.8 mD, Sw=0.18) at 42 and 33 MPa. Case 1 follows a lab-like protocol, while Case 2 lets the mixture relax to minimum Helmholtz energy. Case 2 gave a narrower two-phase mobility region, with condensate mobility threshold 0.28 versus 0.23 in Case 1. The difference is modest because gas-condensate IFT is low, and heavy pseudocomponents concentrate near connate water in small pores.

## Rock Image Enhancement Using Super-Resolution Neural Networks

- ID: SCA2021-028, year: 2021
- Authors: Bo Gong, Paul Duke
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: super-resolution, U-Net, convolutional neural network, BSEM, microCT, pore size distribution
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2021/SCA2021-028.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2021-028)

Super-resolution CNNs (SRCNN, U-Net) enhance low-resolution BSEM and microCT rock images up to 8x, preserving pore edges and pore size distributions better than bicubic interpolation.

Image-derived pore properties depend on resolution, and high-resolution imaging is limited to small samples. The authors trained SRCNN and U-Net models on carbonate BSEM images (0.25 μm, 128×128 sub-images, 65,536 per level), using synthetically down-sampled 2x–16x inputs from one image and a second image as blind test. U-Net mixed models outperformed SRCNN and bicubic interpolation in MSE, and 8x appeared to be the limit of reliable recovery, with the bimodal pore size distribution preserved at 8x and the main peak location at 16x. The workflow was then trained on manually acquired image pairs: BSEM at ~5x resolution difference (86,400 training examples) and microCT at 22.5 and 6.6 μm per voxel (~135,000 samples). The 5x enhanced BSEM image revealed a bimodal pore size distribution, with the microporosity peak consistent with MICP data, and noise was reduced.

## Micromechanics Digital Rock: Parameterization of Consolidation Level using a Grain Contact Model

- ID: SCA2021-033, year: 2021
- Authors: Zhuang Sun, Rafael Salazar-Tio, Andrew Fager, Bernd Crouse
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: cohesive contact model, grain consolidation level, micromechanics digital rock, finite element simulation, elastic moduli, uniaxial compression
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2021/SCA2021-033.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2021-033)

A finite-element digital rock workflow uses a one-parameter cohesive grain contact model to span free to fixed grains, calibrating consolidation level to measured moduli and predicting uniaxial strength.

Micro-CT based digital rock mechanics typically treats grains as one solid frame and overestimates stiffness. The authors add a cohesive contact model to a finite-element grain-segmented workflow, with a single consolidation parameter C (0 = free grains, 1 = fixed grains) linked to the critical separation. The model reproduces free-grain behaviour on a sphere pack (moduli within 3.7% and 3.1%) and fixed-grain behaviour on Grosmont Carbonate (within 1.5%). For a Fontainebleau sandstone model (14.7% porosity), the measured bulk modulus of 22.2 GPa gives C = 0.56. This gives a shear modulus of 24.9 GPa versus 23.5 GPa measured. Normalized modulus versus C follows a logistic function, so only two extra simulations are needed beyond the end members. Uniaxial compression gives Young's modulus of 57 to 90 GPa and UCS of about 98 to 103 MPa, rising with C, with failure by shear band.

## Evaluation of a Numerical Solver (interFoam) to Predict Imbibition and Drainage in a Pore-Doublet System

- ID: SCA2021-040, year: 2021
- Authors: Hosein Derijani, Lesley A. James, Ronald D. Haynes
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: interFoam, OpenFOAM, pore doublet, imbibition and drainage, slip boundary condition, wetting film
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2021/SCA2021-040.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2021-040)

OpenFOAM's interFoam solver is tested on a 2-D pore doublet against Chatzis and Dullien's experiments, qualitatively reproducing imbibition and drainage sequences and wetting-film spreading with slip walls.

The paper checks whether the interFoam two-phase solver (OpenFOAM 5.0) can reproduce pore-scale drainage and imbibition before it is used for digital SCAL. A digitized pore doublet (6.94 x 3.19 x 0.5 mm, 54,230 hexahedral cells) was simulated for n-decane/2% brine and compared with Chatzis and Dullien's visual experiments. Simulations reproduced the expected sequences: imbibition fills the smaller constriction first and drainage invades the larger pore, bypassing wetting fluid in the narrow one. Sensitivity runs on slip versus no-slip walls, dynamic contact angles (45/60 and 10/15 degrees) and wall mesh refinement (107,891 cells, 3D) showed that front instability and snap-off relate to wall slip. Film spreading along walls appeared at very low contact angles and needed slip conditions. Agreement with experiment is qualitative only, and validation on realistic porous media is left for future work.

## THz Imaging to Map the Microporosity Distribution in Carbonate Rocks

- ID: SCA2022-08, year: 2022
- Authors: Shannon L. Eichmann, Jacob Bouchard, Hooisweng Ow, Doug Petkie, Martin Poitzsch
- Method: measurement technique or apparatus
- Keywords: terahertz time-domain spectroscopy, microporosity mapping, carbonate rocks, centrifuge pore clearing, mercury injection capillary pressure, porosity heterogeneity
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2022/SCA2022-08.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2022-08)

THz time-domain spectroscopy imaging of carbonate wafers at saturated, centrifuged and dry states maps microporosity distribution, with microporosity fractions agreeing with mass balance and MICP.

Mapping microporosity (pores <1 µm) over a large field of view is difficult with conventional CT or SEM imaging. The authors imaged half-moon wafers (2, 4, 6 mm thick) of one field carbonate and two Indiana limestone plugs with a Toptica THz-TDS system at 0.5 mm/pixel when saturated, after centrifuging to clear pores larger than 1 µm, and dry. Difference maps of the attenuation give saturated porosity, macroporosity and microporosity maps, revealing distinct spatial patterns such as a central micropore band in Sample 2. Total porosity was 17-20%, and microporosity was 20-50% of total porosity. The microporosity fraction from THz, mass balance and MICP agreed well despite some mass loss from erosion during repeated centrifugation.

## ElRock-Net: Assessing the Utility of Machine Learning to Initialize 3D Electric Potential Simulations

- ID: SCA2022-11, year: 2022
- Authors: Bernard C. Chang, Javier E. Santos, Rodolfo Victor, Maša Prodanović
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: digital rock physics, formation factor, convolutional neural network, electric potential simulation, Laplace equation solver, MS-Net
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2022/SCA2022-11.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2022-11)

A CNN (MS-Net) predicts electric potential in 3D pore images to initialize a finite difference solver, cutting iterations to convergence by over 10x in best cases.

Direct electrical simulations on large digital rock images are costly, while ML predictions do not strictly obey physical constraints. The authors train MS-Net on single 200^3 binary images of four dilated sphere packs and four sandstones (porosity 0.088-0.418), deliberately overfitting for ~30,000 epochs. The predicted, re-trended potential field initializes the Digital Rock Suite Laplace solver, and iterations to convergence are compared with the linear-gradient start. In the best cases iterations fell below 10% of the baseline, and even undertrained models gave over 50% improvement. Predicted formation factor was close to simulation but underpredicted at low porosity with larger uncertainty, due to block artifacts. MSE, PSNR (~40 dB threshold), current-flux uncertainty (~0.1-0.2) and the Laplacian-based D2 score were evaluated as indicators of run time gains.

## Forced Imbibition and Uncertainty Modelling using the Morphological Method

- ID: SCA2022-15, year: 2022
- Authors: Pit Arnold, Mario Dragovits, Sven Linden, Fatime Zekiri, Holger Ott
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: morphological method, forced imbibition, contact angle distribution, digital rock relative permeability, uncertainty modelling, wettability index
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2022/SCA2022-15.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2022-15)

Extends the morphological digital-rock method to full-range forced imbibition using deterministic and stochastic non-wetting regions, giving physical Pc trends, uncertainty ranges and better kr agreement with synchrotron experiments.

The morphological method (MM) for digital rock multiphase flow worked for drainage but handled imbibition poorly and offered no uncertainty analysis. The authors couple spontaneous imbibition with a subsequent forced (drainage-type) step by introducing non-wetting material, either via stochastic Gaussian random fields or deterministically in the largest NWP-filled pores after drainage. Simulations on a Berea digital twin (800³ voxels, 1.84 µm, 19.6% porosity, 780 mD) show that higher solid volume percentage and smaller region size make the system more non-wetting; a 30% SVP case gave log wettability indices of -0.51 (10 µm regions) and 0.34 (100 µm). Relative permeability from the MM was compared with the Berg et al. steady-state synchrotron data on Gildehauser sandstone (20% porosity, 1.55 D simulated). Best matches used about 75% pore space converted and forced imbibition only, avoiding contact angles near 0°, 90° and 180°, while contact angles of 60–120° still fail.

## Digital Rocks Portal (Digital Porous Media): connecting data, simulation and community

- ID: SCA2022-36, year: 2022
- Authors: Maša Prodanović, Maria Esteva, James McClure, Bernard C. Chang, Javier E. Santos, Anuradha Radhakrishnan, Ankita Singh, Hasan Khan
- Method: review or workflow
- Keywords: digital rock physics, data repository, benchmark datasets, machine learning, micro-CT imaging, FAIR data
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2022/SCA2022-36.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2022-36)

Describes the Digital Rocks Portal's data model, usage metrics, benchmark datasets for machine learning, visualization examples and funding model, and its expansion into Digital Porous Media.

The paper presents the Digital Rocks Portal (DRP), a repository for imaged datasets and measurements of porous materials, launched in 2015 and hosted on TACC HPC infrastructure. It describes the data model, curation and DOI publication pipeline, and metrics: 154 projects, 200 related publications, and dataset sizes from 10 MB to 728 GB, with 48% of first authors from the U.S. Community engagement is covered, including newsletters and a visualization mini-course and contest with static image, video and 3D print categories. A benchmark collection of 1736 flow and electrical simulations on 217 digital samples, which took more than 500 core years of computation, supports machine learning training, and Python/PyVista visualization workflows are provided. A sustainability plan introduced publication fees and memberships in September 2021, and the portal is expanding to energy storage materials as Digital Porous Media.

## Towards Multiscale Digital Rocks: Application of a Sub-Resolution Production Model to a multiscale Sandstone

- ID: SCA2022-46, year: 2022
- Authors: Rafael Salazar-Tio, Andrew Fager, Guangyuan Sun, Bernd Crouse, Rui Xu, Brett Wendt, Adam Lewis, Dassault Systèmes
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: lattice Boltzmann, sub-resolution porosity, steady-state relative permeability, USBM wettability, microCT clay-rich sandstone, multiscale digital rock
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 4
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2022/SCA2022-46.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2022-46)

A sub-resolution oil production model, based on USBM wettability data, lets lattice Boltzmann relative permeability simulations on microCT of clay-rich sandstones match lab porosity, permeability and Kr.

Clay-rich sandstones have about half their pore space below microCT resolution, which limits direct-simulation digital rock methods. The authors imaged two tight sandstones (Rock A and B) at 1 µm/voxel, segmented pores and clay, and estimated total porosity from the resolved pores, a Thomeer/MICP-based correction and 50% clay porosity. They ran lattice Boltzmann single-phase and steady-state two-phase simulations in the resolved pores. Unresolved oil was split into water-wet, oil-wet and mix-wet portions using the USBM wettability curve and the simulable capillary pressure limit (7.54 psi), with linear displacement of the mix-wet part, to rescale saturations to the total pore space. Image porosity was within 5% of lab values, simulated permeability was 21.2 vs 19.7 mD (Rock A) and 16.0 vs 14.5 mD (Rock B), and Kr curves were consistent with lab data for Rock A.

## Multi-Scale 3D Carbonate Digital Rock Reconstruction: Traditional or Machine Learning Approaches?

- ID: SCA2022-52, year: 2022
- Authors: Yiteng Li, Xupeng He, Marwa AlSinan, Hyung Kwak, Hussein Hoteit
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: WGAN-GP, variational auto-encoder, multi-scale carbonate, digital rock reconstruction, Minkowski functionals, micro-porosity
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2022/SCA2022-52.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2022-52)

A hybrid WGAN-GP and VAE model reconstructs multi-scale 3D carbonate digital rocks, capturing macro-pores from μ-CT and micro-porosity from SEM, with stable training.

The paper addresses reconstructing multi-scale carbonate digital rocks, where micro-porosity is invisible in μ-CT images. A WGAN-GP is trained to generate macro-porosity, while a VAE extracts fine-scale structure from 2D SEM images through a latent-space probability distribution. An automated workflow covers dataset preparation with subsample extraction and augmentation, model training, and result screening. Synthetic rocks are compared with training samples using two-point autocorrelation, Minkowski functionals, pore size distribution, and simulated permeability, relative permeability and capillary pressure. The combination is reported to reconstruct heterogeneous carbonates well, and WGAN-GP trains stably without heuristics, with the discriminator loss indicating model quality.

## SEM image-constrained process-based modelining for relative permeability estimation of carbonate-rich mudrock

- ID: SCA2022-56, year: 2022
- Authors: Christopher J. Landry, Masa Prodanovic
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: digital rock physics, relative permeability, lattice Boltzmann method, porous solid bitumen, Eagle Ford mudrock, SEM image mosaics
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2022/SCA2022-56.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2022-56)

Uses SEM mosaics to constrain process-based 3D pore models of Eagle Ford mudrock and LBM to estimate oil-water relative permeability, finding a permeability jail.

Steady-state relative permeability measurements on sub-microdarcy mudrocks take weeks to months and reveal little about fluid distribution. The authors use large-area, high-resolution SEM mosaics of a carbonate-rich Eagle Ford lithology to measure porosity, primary porosity, porous solid bitumen (PSB) fraction, and pore size and shape distributions. These constrain process-based 3D models of grain-scale primary pores and PSB-scale pores, which were analysed with lattice Boltzmann simulations for absolute and relative permeability. The primary porosity is well connected alone but obstructed by PSB, and PSB porosity lies below the percolation threshold, so SEM-resolved pores are not connected. Without PSB, models predict a permeability jail at water saturations of 0.42–0.85, 0.46–0.76 and 0.55–0.67 for primary porosity of 11.7, 14.5 and 17.7%.

## A semi-analytical model for capillary entry pressure of pores in carbonates with varying wettability states

- ID: SCA2022-62, year: 2022
- Authors: Yanbin Gong, Bradley William McCaskill, Mohammad Sedghi, Mohammad Piri
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: pore-network modeling, capillary entry pressure, micro-CT, carbonate wettability, pore cross section geometry, piston-like displacement
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2022/SCA2022-62.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2022-62)

A semi-analytical model computes capillary entry pressures from real 2D pore cross sections in micro-CT carbonate networks, showing inscribed radius and shape factor alone are insufficient.

Traditional pore-network models idealize pore geometry, which can cause inaccurate predictions in complex carbonates. The authors couple pore-network modeling with a semi-analytical model that predicts piston-like capillary entry pressure and fluid configuration from real 2D pore images. An algorithm extracts 2D pore and throat cross sections from 3D micro-CT images and is built into an existing network extraction platform, producing enhanced networks for carbonate samples. Entry pressures under different wettability conditions are compared pore by pore with a traditional model. Relying only on inscribed radius and shape factor is insufficient, and other geometric properties such as convexity, solidity and aspect ratio also affect the estimates.

## Multiscale computational models for simulating relative permeability in complex carbonate rock sample

- ID: SCA2023-027, year: 2023
- Authors: Guangyuan Sun, Rafael Salazar-Tio, Jingjing Yang, Hiroshi Otomo, Ganapathi Balasubramanian, Ashraful Islam, Andrew Fager, Bernd Crouse, Raoyang Zhang, Josephina Schembre-McCabe, Dassault Systèmes
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: lattice Boltzmann method, multiscale digital rock, relative permeability, under-resolved porosity, Indiana Limestone, steady-state imbibition
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2023/SCA2023-027.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2023-027)

A multiscale multiphase lattice Boltzmann workflow treats under-resolved pores as porous media with precomputed constitutive curves, matching high-resolution relative permeability and showing under-resolved pores increase oil mobility in carbonates.

Single-scale micro-CT cannot resolve all pore sizes in carbonates, so under-resolved pores are usually ignored in digital-rock relative permeability simulations. The authors extend a multiphase LBM solver so that under-resolved porous-media (PM) voxels carry precomputed k0(phi), Pc(S) and kr(S) relationships, with a Brinkman-type resistance term and capillary force. Steady-state imbibition kr was simulated after an extended Young-Laplace primary drainage at Nc ~1e-5 and 30° contact angle. On a synthetic Fontainebleau-based benchmark, an 8 µm 100^3 multiscale model agreed well with a 1 µm 800^3 fully resolved model (8^3 fewer voxels), including crossover point and residual oil. On Indiana Limestone (about 16% porosity, 7 mD; micrite PM imaged by confocal microscopy at 0.128 µm), multiscale models had higher oil relative permeability than models treating PM as solid, and showed qualitative similarity to a registered high-resolution model in 100^3 and 500^3 subvolumes.

## Quantitative use of dynamic 3D tomography for reservoir, CCS and environmental applications

- ID: SCA2023-048, year: 2023
- Authors: Nicola Bona, Leili Moghadasi, Dario Renna, Martin Barosek, Paola Allegranza, Stefano Soffientini, Laura Dovera, Ernesto Della Rossa, Alberto Guadagnini, Andrea Manzoni, Martina Siena, Aronne Dell'Oca
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: 3D X-ray tomography, core flood history matching, global sensitivity analysis, differential evolution, relative permeability, capillary pressure
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2023/SCA2023-048.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2023-048)

Presents 4D-coreINV, software that history-matches 3D X-ray tomography core floods using GPU simulation, sensitivity analysis and stochastic inversion to estimate 11 parameters with uncertainty.

The paper presents 4D-coreINV, software to simulate and invert core flood experiments monitored by 3D industrial X-ray tomography on cores up to 30 cm long. Saturation maps are acquired at about 50 micron resolution every 3 minutes, with pressure drop logged every 5 seconds over roughly 10-hour experiments. The resulting millions of data points are history-matched to 11 parameters defining porosity-permeability, permeability-irreducible water saturation, initial-residual saturation relationships, capillary pressure and relative permeability curves. Forward runs use the GPU-based reservoir simulator Echelon adapted to lab scale. Morris-based Global Sensitivity Analysis identifies influential parameters, and Differential Evolution minimizes the objective function within a stochastic framework giving parameter probability distributions. The 3D approach is claimed to handle heterogeneous rocks and viscous fingering.

## Synergy between Super-Resolution and Deep Learning: An Application on Thin Sections

- ID: SCA2023-061, year: 2023
- Authors: Xupeng He, Zhen Zhang, Hyung Kwak, Hussein Hoteit
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: super-resolution, WGAN-GP, Attention-UNet, thin sections, deep learning, digital rock
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2023/SCA2023-061.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2023-061)

An Attention-UNet-based WGAN-GP super-resolves low-resolution thin-section images to within 10% error of ground truth, outperforming SRGAN and EDSR on the same dataset.

High-resolution digital rock images take longer to acquire, so image quality trades off against time. The authors propose an Attention-UNet-based Wasserstein GAN with gradient penalty to restore low-resolution thin sections. The workflow uses paired LR and HR lab thin-section images for training, a generator with attention and residual blocks, per-layer loss, and a pre-trained VGG network for feature capture. Predicted HR images are checked against ground truth and match with less than 10% error. On the same dataset it is more accurate than SRGAN and EDSR, which the authors attribute to attention, residual blocks and the more stable WGAN training.

## Digital Rocks Application to Refine Electrical Model of Microporous Carbonates

- ID: SCA2023-064, year: 2023
- Authors: Ivan Deshenenkov, Aqeel Furaish, Hussain Alhilal
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: low resistivity pay, microporous carbonate, Archie saturation exponent, digital rock physics, MICP, drainage relative permeability
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2023/SCA2023-064.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2023-064)

Digital rock models of 62 microporous carbonate plugs simulate electrical flow and drainage relative permeability, showing water-filled micropores mask hydrocarbons in low resistivity pay and enabling refined log saturations.

Low resistivity pay in a microporous carbonate formation leads to overestimated water saturation from logs. The study built 3D digital rock models of samples from 62 core plugs using multi-resolution X-ray tomography and a machine-learning segmentation of microporosity, calibrated and validated against MICP. Numerical simulation of electrical current showed Archie's saturation exponent n varies with water saturation as microporous regions gain influence at lower saturation. Meso- and macropores produce mobile hydrocarbons, while micropores hold immobile water that forms a continuous conductive path. Simulated drainage relative permeability identified first water in-flow saturation, and these results were used to update the wireline saturation profile.

## Federated Learning Test for Collaborative Machine Learning

- ID: SCA2024_1036, year: 2024
- Authors: Bernard Chang, Çinar Turhan, Maria Esteva, Maša Prodanović, Jeremy T. First, Yang Ning, Yuliana Zapata, Glen Gettemy, Caio Graco Pereira Santos, Jonatas Castro Einsielder
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: federated learning, MS-Net, digital rock physics, permeability prediction, Minkowski functionals, lattice-Boltzmann
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2024/SCA2024_1036.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2024_1036)

Federated learning of MS-Net across bp, Petrobras and UT predicts permeability from rock images without sharing data; federated client models generalized better than locally trained ones.

Data sharing barriers hinder training of deep learning models for digital rock physics, so the authors tested federated learning (FL) with MS-Net, which predicts velocity fields and permeability from segmented 3D images. Three clients (bp, Petrobras, UT Austin) each prepared at least 100 images of 256^3 voxels, computed LBPM velocity fields with identical settings, and shared only summary descriptors: Minkowski functionals, a heterogeneity classification curve and a morphological-drainage crossover saturation (≤0.40 at a 3-voxel throat radius). Training used FedAvg via Flower with manual aggregation, 10 rounds of 10 local epochs. On 25 blind DRP sandstone samples, all federated client models had lower permeability MSE than local-only models (bp 1.70e-8 to 5.39e-9, Petrobras 3.71e-8 to 2.33e-8, UT 5.82e-9 to 3.05e-9). The aggregated server model fell between clients, reflecting non-IID data and the FedAvg weighting toward Petrobras, the largest contributor (40% of samples). Loss magnitudes indicate further training is needed, and better aggregation strategies, retained optimizer states and tests on other lithologies are future work.

## Digital Rocks Portal for image curation, characterization, visualization, and transport simulation in porous media

- ID: SCA2024_1056, year: 2024
- Authors: Çınar Turhan, Bernard Chang, Ali Mohamed, Maria Esteva, Richard Ketcham, James McClure, Maša Prodanović
- Method: review or workflow
- Keywords: Digital Rocks Portal, FAIR data model, lattice Boltzmann LBPM, competent subset selection, Minkowski functionals, data reuse citation analysis
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2024/SCA2024_1056.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2024_1056)

Describes the Digital Rocks Portal's updated data model, Python analysis and visualization tools, LBPM simulation integration, and a citation analysis of 22 papers reusing its datasets.

The Digital Rocks Portal (DRP), holding 162 porous media datasets, is being rebuilt on TACC infrastructure and renamed Digital Porous Media Portal. A v2.0 data model adds metadata such as related datasets, related software, energy storage media, sample source types and imaging equipment. New open-source Python tools include visualization (PyVista), Minkowski functionals, heterogeneity classification, competent subset selection, medial axis extraction, an image processing workflow, and the LBPM lattice Boltzmann solver with a pyLBPM interface. A citation analysis of 22 randomly selected 2020–2023 papers found about 65% of reused data were CT or micro-CT, 55% involved image processing, and 41% were reuse by others and 18% reuse by the original authors. Datasets were mostly used for simulation (35%), then machine learning (26%), experiments (22%) and characterization (17%). Tools will become containerized HPC applications, with sustainability supported by TACC's Core Experience Portal.

## Physically based neural networks for solving transport problems in porous media

- ID: SCA2024_1084, year: 2024
- Authors: Saeid Khasi, Apostolos Kantzas
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: convolutional neural network, finite difference, diffusion equation, GPU parallel computing, input optimization, transport in porous media
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2024/SCA2024_1084.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2024_1084)

Encodes implicit finite-difference diffusion/convection discretization as CNN kernels with input optimization; on GPU it beats implicit FDM runtime at 500×500 grids with comparable mass conservation.

The paper replaces finite-difference matrix solving for transport in porous media with a convolutional neural network whose kernels are the Laplacian (five-point stencil) from the implicit discretization. The concentration field is the trainable input layer, optimized with Adam (learning rate 0.01, tolerance 0.001, up to 500 iterations) against a squared-residual loss, with Dirichlet boundaries imposed by padding. For 2D diffusion, mass-conservation error over 10 steps was 0.7% for explicit FDM, 0.26% for implicit FDM and 0.28% for the CNN. On a Tesla T4 GPU, CNN runtime levelled off at about 70 s from 200×200 to 500×500, whereas implicit FDM rose to 133 s at 500×500. Adding convection needs only two gradient kernels, and higher Peclet number produced higher loss, implying higher computational cost. LSTM/ConvLSTM extensions and surrogate use are proposed but not tested.

## Digital SCAL in Complex Carbonates by the Density Functional Hydrodynamics Method

- ID: SCA_2025_1013, year: 2025
- Authors: Denis Klemin, Shehadeh Masalmeh, Ali AlSumaiti, Ritesh Bhakta, Moustafa Dernaika
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: digital SCAL, density functional hydrodynamics, relative permeability, mixed-wet carbonates, contact angle wettability, multi-scale microCT SEM
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2025/SCA_2025_1013.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA_2025_1013)

Blind-test validation of Density Functional Hydrodynamics digital SCAL on two Abu Dhabi carbonates: wettability-constrained pore-scale simulations predicted steady-state waterflood relative permeability that matched measurements well.

Digital rock predictions of two-phase flow in mixed-wet carbonates are limited by wettability and network uncertainty. Four dolomite and limestone plugs (porosity 14-30%, permeability 40-60 mD) were imaged with plug CT, microCT (~1.3-1.5 µm) and SEM/AMICS to build multi-scale digital rock models. Models were validated against measured porosity, permeability (within 3%, except 18A at 11%) and MICP. Contact angle (105°) was constrained by drainage and imbibition Pc, and DHD simulations were run blind for dolomite 18A and limestone 23A. Digital imbibition Kr curves matched steady-state measurements well, especially for 23A. The 18A Krw mismatch beyond Sw 0.5 is attributed to a capillary end effect in the lab test. A wettability sensitivity run on an outcrop showed expected trends in Kr and Sor.

## On-site FTIR analysis for accurate mineralogical screening of cuttings and core: a case study of the Boom Clay

- ID: SCA_2025_1020, year: 2025
- Authors: Rieko Adriaens, Hervé Van Baelen, Bram Paredis, Noël Vandenberghe, Gilles Mertens
- Method: measurement technique or apparatus
- Keywords: FTIR spectroscopy, mineralogy quantification, Boom Clay, XRD calibration, cuttings analysis, well log correlation
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2025/SCA_2025_1020.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA_2025_1020)

An XRD-calibrated, automated ATR-FTIR method gives minutes-fast on-site mineralogy and TOC of Boom Clay cuttings and cores, closely matching XRD and clarifying well-log interpretation.

On-site mineralogical screening is needed to guide site characterization for radioactive waste disposal in the Boom Clay, where XRD is too slow and lab-bound. The authors developed an automated FTIR-based High Speed Mineralogical Quantification (HSMQ) model calibrated against XRD, using a Bruker Alpha II ATR spectrometer on milled powders (<90 µm) with 6-7 minutes from milling to results. It was tested on the Rumst outcrop (N=269), Essen-1 core (N=689) and Mol-2D core shoe samples and cuttings (N=315 and 67). Against XRD, quartz deviated about 1.5% on average, clays 2-4%, feldspars 0.5-0.8%, and trace minerals were quantified down to about 1%. The data identified key septarian horizons, glauconitic sands, organic-rich layers and kaolinite markers, explained gamma-ray and resistivity logs, and suggested adjustments to some log-based stratigraphic boundaries.

## Laboratory validation of geoelectrical models for permeability prediction

- ID: SCA_2025_1059, year: 2025
- Authors: Andreas Weller, Klaudio Peshtani, Mohamed Kassab, Lee Slater
- Method: data analysis or correlation
- Keywords: capillary bundle model, formation factor, spectral induced polarization, permeability prediction, effective hydraulic radius, flow zone indicator
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2025/SCA_2025_1059.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA_2025_1059)

Using 282 sandstone, carbonate and mudstone samples, the study tests capillary-bundle-based geoelectrical permeability models, finding formation factor key and imaginary conductivity useful only with formation-specific calibration.

Permeability prediction from geophysical data is difficult, so the authors test capillary bundle model (CBM) based geoelectrical models on a database of 282 samples from 12 sandstone, carbonate and mudstone formations. Permeability spans 3e-20 to 8e-12 m², formation factor 4.3 to 9880, and spectral induced polarization gives the imaginary conductivity at 1 Hz. No general porosity-permeability relationship exists; power-law exponents range from near zero to 15, though CBM explains the exponents for mudstones (1.49) and Fontainebleau (5.62). Six of twelve formations show good k–F power laws, but the exponent β varies between formations (about 1 to 5). A prediction model using F and imaginary conductivity (k = a_q/(F³σ''²)) reproduces trends only with formation-specific a_q, and a uniform a_q overestimates Fontainebleau and underestimates Sherwood. RQI and FZI correlate strongly with k but need measured k, and no formation has constant FZI.

## Dual-Wetting Porous Plate System to Measure Drainage and Imbibition Capillary Pressure: A Case Study on a Complex Reservoir Carbonate

- ID: SCA2026-008, year: 2026
- Authors: Franck Nono, Titly Farhana Faisal, Nacer Benlalam, Isabelle Jolivet, Clément Varloteaux, Mohamed Regaieg, Georgy Borisochev, Abbas Zerkoune, Cyril Caubit
- Method: measurement technique or apparatus
- Keywords: dual porous plate, capillary pressure, imbibition, unresolved porosity, micro-CT saturation monitoring, carbonate wettability
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 7
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2026/SCA2026-008.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2026-008)

A dual-wetting porous plate cell with micro-CT measures full drainage-imbibition Pc cycles on one mini plug of complex carbonate, matching SCAL results and revealing wettability heterogeneity.

Digital rock wettability workflows work poorly for complex rocks with large unresolved porosity, and need full capillary pressure cycles on one sample. The authors built an X-ray transparent cell with a water-wet bottom and an oil-wet top alumina porous plate, enabling primary drainage, ageing with crude oil, and positive and negative imbibition without dismantling. It was tested on a ~20 mD calcite carbonate (about 75% of 29.5 p.u. porosity unresolved) using a 1 cm mini plug, and compared with an unsteady-state SCAL experiment on a 5 cm plug inverted numerically. Primary drainage Pc agreed closely with SCAL (Swi 7.2 vs 8 s.u.), and imbibition in the more homogeneous top half matched SCAL, while the bottom half differed. Drainage-defined throat-size subregions in the unresolved porosity showed different brine uptake in spontaneous imbibition (normalized saturation 20.0% large, 17.9% medium, 31.1% small), indicating wettability heterogeneity.

## 3D Reconstruction of Super-Resolved Micro-CT Images of Kaolinitefilled Sandstones

- ID: SCA2026-033, year: 2026
- Authors: Pit Arnold, Viswambhar Reddy Yasa, Mayank Nagar, Till Bollermann, Mohsen Rafiee, Wesam Ben Mansour, Andrew Fogden
- Method: digital rock or imaging
- Keywords: CycleGAN super-resolution, kaolinite microporosity, digital core analysis, micro-CT and SEM, pore network modelling, gas-water relative permeability
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 1
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2026/SCA2026-033.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2026-033)

Two CycleGAN models turn 64³ micro-CT kaolinite volumes into 1000³ super-resolved cubes, whose simulated flow properties augment digital core analysis of tight gas sandstones, giving trapped gas consistent with lab trends.

Standard micro-CT digital core analysis fails for tight, clay-rich sandstones because kaolinite microporosity is unresolved. The authors trained two CycleGAN models on unpaired micro-CT crops (2.33 µm voxels) and SEM BSE crops (0.25 µm pixels) from Norwegian Continental Shelf sandstones. Combined with interpolation, alignment and median blending, the workflow converts 64³ voxel cubes into 1000³ cubes at 0.146 µm, a 16-fold resolution increase. Thirty-nine cubes were generated with porosity 11–56% and lattice-Boltzmann permeability 0.31–120 mD, strongly power-law correlated with porosity. Pore-network gas-water relative permeability and capillary pressure were simulated for each. In a gas-well case study of 9 plugs, augmented DCA on 4 tight plugs (0.1–1 mD) gave imbibition endpoint Swrg near the lab-fitted P50 trapped-gas trend and consistent with standard DCA on the more permeable plugs.

## Single-Phase Greyscale Lattice Boltzmann Simulation for Porous Media

- ID: SCA2026-037, year: 2026
- Authors: Omid Tavakkoli, Ying Da Wang, Benzhong Zhao, Peyman Mostaghimi, Zhe Li, Ryan T. Armstrong
- Method: numerical simulation
- Keywords: greyscale lattice Boltzmann, Kozeny–Carman constant, Darcy–Brinkman equation, micro-CT microporosity, Bandera Brown sandstone, resolution dependence
- Cited by 0 papers in the corpus, cites 0
- [PDF](https://www.jgmaas.com/SCA/2026/SCA2026-037.pdf) · [On the site](https://mralbu.github.io/sca-papers/#SCA2026-037)

A greyscale lattice Boltzmann framework with Kozeny–Carman closure simulates flow on unsegmented micro-CT images, matches binary results within 1%, and shows C scales with resolution.

Binary segmentation of micro-CT images discards sub-voxel porosity and forces a trade-off between resolution and field of view. The authors develop a Guo-type D3Q19 MRT greyscale LBM with voxel porosity and Kozeny–Carman permeability, derive its reduction to the Darcy–Brinkman equation, and verify it on parallel plates, a linear porosity gradient and coarsened Bentheimer sandstone (7.71% difference from binary reference). A linear-gradient test exposes a 16–20% bias versus the harmonic average, which largely cancels in disordered sandstones. On Bandera Brown sandstone, greyscale LBM at 3.6 µm gave 427 mD versus 423 mD for binary at 0.7 µm on a co-registered sub-volume, while on the full domain greyscale gave 259 mD versus 500 mD binary. Coarsening to 28.8 µm at fixed C overestimated permeability by 161%; the required C fell from 2.67 to 0.44 µm², with √C linear in voxel size (R²=0.987).
